I provided the prompt following for cursor to plan out the next phase (3): ```# SESSION CHARTER — AI Brain Hub + Self-Improving Loop (design → Fable prompt)

You are Cursor Agent (Grok 4.5). Mode: **Agent**. This is a **design + clarification session**, not a Fable coding dump.

## North star

Build the **main AI hub** for this personal US stocks/ETFs research desk (`research_data` in `/home/anant_gupta/projects/hub/tradingview`).

The brain already exists as a **closed research loop** (Python facts + human gates). What is missing is the **AI harness**: thin analyst/critic that only cites packets, plus optional LLM **proposals** that cite vault/papers and **never invent numbers** — always **human-gated**.

Locked product law (do not reopen unless a question forces an explicit supersede):
- Personal desk only; stocks/ETFs only; no PM/Kalshi in this vertical.
- Python owns facts/factors/gates/paper fills. AI proposes/explains from evidence only.
- Action vocab only: `WATCH | HOLD | ACCUMULATE | REDUCE | AVOID | INSUFFICIENT_DATA`.
- No `BUY`/`SELL`/"guaranteed"/"risk-free".
- Four gates fixed: OOS → Monte Carlo → walk-forward → deflated Sharpe (literature defaults).
- Brain loop: `citation → proposed spec → human approve → Python hook → four gates → promote/demote → journal lesson → next proposal`.
- Approver identity: `anant`. AI may propose; AI must never approve/promote.
- Phase 2b just landed: `[REDACTED]` is **demo_eligible** on real tiingo depth (PR #3 / main). Self-evolving AI work starts **because** a promotion-grade artifact exists — not before dumping freeform “knowledge” into the brain (student-toy failure mode, rejected).

## What “self-improving” means here (correct framing)

Not: LLM rewrites strategy math, loosens gates, or invents edge.
Yes: auditable compound loop —
1. Deterministic packets (prices, quality, ScorePacket, gate TestRunRecords, journal lessons).
2. Analyst summarizes **only** what packets say.
3. Critic attacks thesis / flags contradiction / caps confidence by quality status.
4. Optional proposer emits a **StrategySpec draft** with required Citation IDs (vault notes, papers, journal lessons) — still `PROPOSED` until `anant` approves.
5. Python hook + gates decide truth; journal lesson becomes the next citable input.

Inspiration only (architecture patterns, not copy): TradingAgents role separation; agency-agents role contracts; vault roadmap “Compound / Learn & Improve”. Do **not** copy multi-agent trade debate as edge.

3. Every question must include: **context (why it matters)**, **A–E options that are researched/realistic**, **recommended default**, **what a bad choice breaks**.

## Questionnaire agenda (cover all; order flexible; one-at-a-time)

For each question, research options against: Bailey/LdP multiple-testing discipline, evidence-bound LLM systems, TradingAgents-style role separation (inspiration only), existing `brain` schemas, and the rejected student-toy “dump knowledge then hope” pattern.

### Block A — Scope & success bar
**A1. What is V1 of the AI hub?**  
Options research: (A) symbol evidence-card CLI only (`analyze_symbol` → JSON card), (B) analyst+critic on demo_eligible specs only, (C) full proposer that drafts StrategySpecs from journal lessons, (D) A+B, (E) A+B+C thin path.  
Default lean: **D**, then C as V1.1 after card quality eval.

**A2. What closes the “self-improving” loop in V1?**  
(A) journal lesson → Citation only (manual propose), (B) LLM proposer emits PROPOSED specs citing lessons, (C) auto-approve low-risk param tweaks (reject unless you explicitly want this — default **no**), (D) critic-only demotion suggestions for human HOLD/DEMOTE, (E) B+D.

**A3. Demo / desk-is-real bar for AI slice**  
(A) offline synthetic packet → card golden tests, (B) live packet for one demo_eligible holding (e.g. NVDA/GOOGL/AAPL from study) → card, (C) full watchlist digest, (D) B + one human-gated proposal draft, (E) UI required (default **no**).

### Block B — Packet contracts (facts the LLM may see)
**B1. Minimum packet set for analyst input**  
ScorePacket / DataEvidencePacket / gate TestRunRecords / paper JournalEntry / FundamentalsSnapshot / Kronos reserved (must stay unused) — which subset is mandatory vs optional?

**B2. Evidence card schema**  
Evolve vault YAML (`Trading with Ai`) vs new Pydantic `EvidenceCard` in repo? Versioning? Where stored (DuckDB table vs `data/cards/` JSON)?

**B3. Number invention tripwire**  
How enforce: (A) LLM may only quote numbers present in input JSON (regex/post-validator), (B) structured fill-in template with slots, (C) critic rejects any numeric token not in allowlist from packets, (D) A+C, (E) trust prompts only (reject).

**B4. Confidence capping**  
Wire QualityStatus precedence into card confidence the same way factors do? Critic may only lower, never raise?

### Block C — Agent topology (thin, not a swarm)
**C1. Roles in V1**  
(A) single Analyst, (B) Analyst + Critic, (C) + Risk Critic separate, (D) + Tutor student voice, (E) TradingAgents-scale bull/bear/trader (default **too much**).

**C2. Orchestration**  
(A) plain Python functions + optional LLM client, (B) LangGraph/Crew-style graph, (C) CLI subcommands only, (D) Cursor skills as the “agents”, (E) A+C.

**C3. Model provider**  
(A) no live LLM in CI — fixtures only; live optional via env, (B) OpenAI, (C) Anthropic, (D) local Ollama, (E) pluggable provider interface with offline default. Prefer E.

**C4. When may LLM run?**  
Never in: ingestion, factors, gates, normalization. Confirm path boundaries and import lints/tests.

### Block D — Proposal / citation logistics (the real self-improve seam)
**D1. Citation ingest**  
How do vault notes / papers become `Citation` rows? Manual CLI? Hash of path+mtime? Claim extraction human-edited vs LLM-drafted claims (still human approve)?

**D2. StrategySpec proposal contract**  
Required fields beyond today’s model? Must include: hook_ref candidates? param deltas only vs new hooks? max n_trials accounting for DSR?

**D3. Forbidden proposal classes in V1**  
Gate constant changes, universe expansion, cost model edits, Kronos promote influence, execution language — confirm reject list.

**D4. Human gate UX**  
CLI `brain propose/approve/reject`? Markdown PR in vault? Both? Approver string validation already exists — extend how?

**D5. Lesson → next proposal**  
Automatic link from `JournalEntry` close (with `voo_return_same_period`) into Citation `journal_lesson`? Who triggers proposer?

### Block E — Eval, safety, cost
**E1. Offline eval harness**  
Golden packets → card schema validation; contradiction injection; missing-data must yield `INSUFFICIENT_DATA`; citation hallucination test.

**E2. Live eval**  
Blind compare analyst card vs study stdout numbers; critic must catch planted false Sharpe.

**E3. Secrets & cost**  
API keys in `.env` only; max tokens; no packet contents in cloud logs; redaction policy.

**E4. Calibration later**  
Brier / action-outcome tracking — V1 stub table vs defer?

### Block F — Repo / vault structure
**F1. Code home**  
`src/research_data/agents/` vs `ai/` vs `brain/ai/`? Keep brain store pure (no LLM imports)?

**F2. Docs SoT naming**  
`[REDACTED].md` + `SOLUTION_DESIGN.md` pattern?

**F3. Vault rearrange plan**  
Propose Canon / Decisions / Phases / Research / Distilled mirrors under TradingView; banners; update Fable read-order note; mark Post-Base superseded by new Session Findings after this questionnaire.

### Block G — Cursor vs Fable split + sequencing
**G1. Cursor implements before Fable** (check all that apply after locks): EvidenceCard schema + DuckDB table; citation import CLI; `analyze_symbol` packet assembler (no LLM); offline validators; Docs SoT; vault folders; CI guard that agents/ cannot import providers.

**G2. Fable one-shot scope**  
Analyst+critic prompts + runner + tests + one live demo card on demo_eligible path + optional proposer — confirm cut line.

**G3. Explicit non-goals for this phase**  
UI/Streamlit, Kronos RankIC, live broker, auto-promote, news/sentiment fetch, multi-agent debate theater.

## B1 — Minimum packet set for analyst / critic input

**Why it matters:** The LLM may only see structured packets. Too little → invents factor/gate numbers. Too much (raw OHLCV dumps, Kronos, news) → cost, leakage, and fake precision. A3 needs both a **symbol card** path and a **spec critic** path; they share a core but aren’t identical.

| Packet | Role today | V1 lean |
|---|---|---|
| `DataEvidencePacket` | Price/quality provenance + `confidence_cap` | Mandatory (symbol) |
| `ScorePacket` | Factor math + embedded quality/valuation/TA context | Mandatory (symbol) |
| `TestRunRecord` batch | Gate pass/fail + outputs | Mandatory for demo_eligible critic |
| `PromotionDecision` | Human promote/hold/demote + rationale ids | Mandatory for critic artifact |
| `JournalEntry` | Exits/lessons (+ `voo_return_same_period` on exit) | Mandatory for A2/A3 citation/critic |
| `FundamentalsSnapshot` | Raw statement rows | Optional — already partially in `ScorePacket.quality_fcf` |
| Kronos reserved | Schema only; no inference | **Forbidden** as analyst input |

| | Option | Mandatory set | Optional |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | `DataEvidencePacket` only | Prices/quality | Everything else omitted → invents scores |
| **B** | `ScorePacket` + `DataEvidencePacket` | Symbol card floor | No gate/journal → critic demotion is theater |
| **C** | **B** + latest gate `TestRunRecord` batch | Symbol + gate facts | Weak on “why human promoted” / lessons |
| **D** | **C** + `PromotionDecision` + relevant `JournalEntry`s (exit/lesson/holdings for that spec) | Full A1–A3 evidence bound | Raw `FundamentalsSnapshot` only if ScorePacket marks `INSUFFICIENT_DATA` |
| **E** | **D** + full OHLCV series + Kronos + news | Kitchen sink | Violates Kronos lock; cost/hallucination |

**Recommended default: D.** Symbol path always gets ScorePacket + DataEvidencePacket. Spec critic path adds latest gate batch + PromotionDecision + journal rows for that `spec_id`. FundamentalsSnapshot not required when ScorePacket components are `OK`; Kronos never attached.

**Bad choice breaks:**
- **A/B** → critic can’t cite DSR/WF/journal; demotion suggestions become vibes.
- **C** → misses human decision + lesson citations A2 requires.
- **E** → Kronos/news/raw bars invite invention and burn tokens; fails product law.

## Claude

Let me look at the actual data models before evaluating this — I want to see what's really in each packet, not just trust the summary table.

## What B1 is actually asking

Right now the AI hub (per your A1/A2/A3 answers) has two jobs: write a **symbol evidence card** (e.g. "here's what the data says about NVDA") and write a **critic artifact** that can suggest HOLD/DEMOTE on the one `demo_eligible` spec (`[REDACTED]`). Both jobs are LLM calls. The guardrail in `CLAUDE.md` is absolute: *"AI agents must cite timestamped evidence... they must not invent metrics, prices, or recommendations."*

B1 is deciding **exactly which JSON objects get pasted into that LLM's prompt** — not vibes, the literal `.model_dump_json()` payloads. Too little and the model has no numbers to work with, so it hallucinates a Sharpe ratio or a rank. Too much (raw price series, Kronos, news) and you're paying tokens for noise and inviting the model to "recompute" things itself instead of quoting what's already computed — which is exactly the failure mode the whole packet architecture exists to prevent.

## What's actually in each packet (I read the code, not just the table)

| Packet | What it really holds |
|---|---|
| `DataEvidencePacket` (`evidence.py`+`models.py:269`) | **No prices.** Just provenance: source, date window, `quality_status`, `confidence_cap`, `missing_sessions`, and `evidence_refs` (pointers into `daily_ohlcv`/`data_quality_reports` with hash + retrieved_at). This is the *citation* object. |
| `ScorePacket` (`factors/packets.py`) | The actual numbers: momentum rank + 12-1 return, safety rank, quality_fcf composite **and its raw components** (fcf_ev, fcf_margin, debt/equity, etc.), valuation context, ETF-baseline comparison, TA context, `risk_flags`, plus its **own** `data_quality` (status + max_confidence) and `provenance` block. |
| `TestRunRecord` (`brain/models.py:160`) | One gate run. `inputs`/`outputs` are typed as `dict[str, Any]` — **freeform**. I checked each gate module: OOS, Monte Carlo, walk-forward, and deflated-Sharpe each write their own ad-hoc key names into `outputs` (e.g. `deflated_sharpe.py` writes `"deflated_sharpe_probability"`, `walk_forward.py` writes `"windows"`). There's no shared contract across the four gates for what keys exist. |
| `PromotionDecision` (`brain/models.py:186`) | The human's actual promote/hold/demote call, with `rationale` (required, non-empty) and lists of which `TestRunRecord`/`Citation`/`JournalEntry` ids justified it. |
| `JournalEntry` (`paper/models.py:112`) | Exit entries are **schema-enforced** to carry `voo_return_same_period` (a `model_validator` raises if you try to close a position without the benchmark number). |
| `FundamentalsSnapshot` | Raw statement rows. But `ScorePacket.quality_fcf_score.components` already surfaces the *derived* fcf/margin/debt numbers with `fundamentals_as_of`/`fundamentals_source`. The raw snapshot adds nothing an LLM needs — it's a human-audit object, not an LLM-input object. |
| Kronos | Its own model (`kronos_reserved.py`) makes constructing invalid evidence a `ValueError` at the type level (must be `USABLE` quality + RankIC ≥ 0.03, which has never been validated). It's not just "forbidden by policy," it's currently **impossible to instantiate legitimately**. |

## Is D actually right?

Yes, for the scope you already locked in A2/A3 — but with two things worth fixing before you send it back, because Cursor's table glossed over both:

**1. Real redundancy between `DataEvidencePacket` and `ScorePacket`.** `ScorePacket.data_quality` and `.provenance` already duplicate most of what `DataEvidencePacket` says (quality status, confidence cap, source, price dates). The reason to still include `DataEvidencePacket` isn't the data — it's `evidence_refs`, which is the only object with actual citeable ids (hash + retrieved_at) matching the CLAUDE.md line naming it as *the* citation source. Tell Cursor to make that explicit: **the analyst prompt should quote `evidence_refs` for citations and pull the numbers from `ScorePacket` alone** — not serialize both packets' overlapping quality blocks and hope the model doesn't notice they could theoretically disagree.

**2. `TestRunRecord.outputs` is an untyped grab-bag, and that's a real hallucination-adjacent risk for the critic, not a hypothetical one.** Since each gate module invents its own output keys with no shared schema, "the critic may only cite numbers present in the packet" (your B3 question, coming up next) is unenforceable against a `dict[str, Any]` — a renamed key or a gate you haven't looked at closely could silently drop a number the prompt template expects. Before locking D, I'd add a requirement: **B1's "TestRunRecord batch" should mean a fixed, whitelisted projection** (e.g. `oos_net_sharpe`, `mc_p5_annualized_return`, `wf_pct_positive_windows`, `deflated_sharpe_probability` — the four keys that actually exist today across the four gate files) rather than the raw dict, so the critic's citeable surface is closed, not open-ended.

Neither of these changes the answer — **D is still the right set** — but as written it lets two things slide (double-source-of-truth on quality, and an open-ended dict as if it were structured) that will bite when you get to B3 (number-invention tripwire), where enforcing "only cite what's in the packet" requires the packet to actually be closed-shape.

**My suggested reply to Cursor:** *"D, with two amendments: (1) analyst quotes ScorePacket for numbers and DataEvidencePacket only for evidence_refs/citations — don't serialize both quality blocks; (2) TestRunRecord input to the critic must be a fixed key whitelist (oos_net_sharpe, mc_p5_return, wf_pct_positive, deflated_sharpe_probability), not the raw inputs/outputs dict."*

## Claude

I checked how this repo actually handles DuckDB schema changes before answering — it changes the calculus here.

**What I found:**
- Every existing store (`storage.py`, `brain/store.py`, `paper/store.py`) uses the exact same pattern: `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` run once at init, called `init_schema()`/`init_db()`. There is **no migration mechanism anywhere** — no `ALTER TABLE`, no versioned migrations.
- `data/` has `market.duckdb.bak-20260711` and `market.duckdb.bak-phase2b-20260711` — confirming schema changes here are handled by **backup-then-rebuild the whole DB file**, not in-place migration.
- The exact "file on disk + hash-keyed pointer row in DuckDB" shape option D wants already exists: `raw_market_payloads` (`storage.py:88`) has `raw_payload_hash PRIMARY KEY`, `payload_path VARCHAR` pointing at the file on disk, plus provenance columns. So D isn't inventing a new pattern — it's reusing one that's already proven.
- Buried in Cursor's own option table: **"Recommended default: B for V1... Add DuckDB table in V1.1"** — Cursor already recommended sequencing, it just phrased it as a footnote instead of the headline.

**What this means for your instinct:** you're right to split it, and it's not just caution for caution's sake — there's a concrete mechanical reason. `EvidenceCard`'s shape is going to move once you actually run the golden tests and the one live card (A3). If you commit to the DuckDB table on day one (D as a single build), every shape tweak during that stabilization period means rebuilding the DB file too (per this repo's own no-migration convention) — you'd be doing schema surgery on two artifacts instead of one while the shape is still moving. That's the same risk Cursor flagged for option C ("schema migrations before card shape stabilizes") — it just as clearly applies to D's DB half, since D also creates that table immediately.

Once the JSON files stabilize (golden tests green + the live card from A3 doesn't need a breaking field change), adding the DuckDB mirror is genuinely cheap and low-risk: read an already-validated JSON file, hash it, insert one pointer row — copy `raw_market_payloads`'s exact shape (`card_id`/hash `PRIMARY KEY`, `card_path`, `symbol`, `as_of`, `schema_version`, nullable `spec_id`). There's no new design risk left to resolve at that point.

**My answer: B now, with the DuckDB mirror locked in as an explicit second build (not a new open question) — sequenced, not parallel.**

Suggested reply to Cursor:

> **B2: B** — `src/research_data/cards/models.py`, Pydantic `EvidenceCard` + `schema_version`, CLI writes `data/cards/{symbol}_{as_of}_{card_id}.json`, offline goldens on fixtures. Vault YAML becomes a mirror example, not SoT. `CriticReview` is a separate model from the symbol card, not jammed in.
>
> **Sequencing note (not reopening the option):** the DuckDB `evidence_cards` table (option D's second half) is build #2, started only after golden tests pass and the live A3 card hasn't required a breaking field change — reuse `raw_market_payloads`'s exact hash+path-pointer shape (`card_id` PK, `card_path`, `symbol`, `as_of`, `schema_version`, nullable `spec_id`). This repo has no migration mechanism (schema changes = backup + rebuild the DuckDB file per the existing `.bak-*` files), so committing the table before the card shape stabilizes means rebuilding two artifacts instead of one during iteration.

## B3 — Number-invention tripwire

**Why it matters:** Product law is “AI never invents numbers.” Prompts alone fail under adversarial eval (planted false Sharpe, extra decimals). With B1’s whitelist + ScorePacket-only numerics, the allowlist of legal numeric tokens is finite and machine-checkable. This is the main offline eval that makes A3’s golden suite real.

| | Option | Mechanism | Strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Post-validator: every number in card/review text+fields must appear in input JSON (normalized float/int match) | Regex/AST over serialized card vs packet allowlist | Strong; some false positives on dates/ids |
| **B** | Structured fill-in only: LLM fills string slots; all floats come from deterministic templates | LLM never emits free numerics | Strongest for scores; weak for prose “Sharpe was high” hedging |
| **C** | Critic rejects any numeric token not in allowlist (second pass) | Adversarial role | Good; needs A or shared allowlist builder |
| **D** | **A + C** — build allowlist from ScorePacket + GateSummaryProjection + journal floats; post-validate analyst card; critic must fail planted extras | Defense in depth | Matches A1 analyst+critic |
| **E** | Trust system prompts only | Cheap | **Reject** — fails citation/number hallucination tests |

**Recommended default: D.** Shared `NumericAllowlist` from assembler (ScorePacket numbers + four gate whitelist keys + journal `realized_return` / `voo_return_same_period`). Analyst card must pass allowlist check before write. CriticReview must either introduce no new numbers or only quote allowlisted ones; planted false Sharpe in fixtures must fail closed. Dates/UUIDs/schema_version excluded via typed fields, not free-text scraping of those fields.

**Bad choice breaks:**
- **E** → first live card invents a Sharpe; you won’t catch it in CI.
- **B alone** → prose still smuggles numbers (“up ~35%”) unless slots cover every claim.
- **A without C** → single validator bug = silent pass; critic becomes decorative.
- **C without A** → critic LLM might “approve” hallucinated numbers if the critic itself isn’t bound.

Reply **A / B / C / D / E**. Then **B4**.```. Agree with cursor here for option d but it needs a note based on the actual truth of the codebase. Let's add a note to the reply in a manner that we are more clear with the option chosen.

## Claude

I checked whether option D's post-validator (component A: "every number in card text must appear in input JSON, normalized float/int match") actually holds up against how this repo already formats numbers — and it doesn't, without a fix.

**What I found:** this codebase already has an established convention of rounding floats for any human-facing text — `walk_forward.py:113` writes `fraction_positive={fraction_positive:.2f}`, `cli.py:370` writes `confidence_cap={report.confidence_cap:.2f}`, `benchmark.py:73` writes `{self.benchmark_excess_return:.6f}`. None of these render the raw float. That means an `EvidenceCard`'s prose is always going to say something like *"12-1 return of 14.3%"* while `ScorePacket.momentum_score.twelve_minus_one_return` holds `0.142876581...` — a literal/exact string match between card text and packet JSON will **never** pass, even for a fully honest card. Option A as worded ("normalized float/int match") is ambiguous about this, and if Cursor implements it as exact-string containment, the very first golden test fails on a true card — which is exactly the kind of validator-bug-as-silent-pass risk D's own "Bad choice breaks" section warns about for A-without-C, just showing up one layer earlier.

There's a second, smaller wrinkle: I found `ScorePacket` has several **structural int fields** that will legitimately appear in card prose but aren't "claims" the way a Sharpe or return is — `rank`, `universe_size`, `ranked_count`, `window_sessions`, `overlapping_sessions`, `price_rows_used`. These need exact-match (no tolerance), while the float performance numbers need tolerance-based matching. Treating both classes the same way in the allowlist builder is where a first implementation is likely to get either too strict (rejects honest rounding) or too loose (tolerance so wide it stops catching planted false numbers).

Suggested reply to Cursor:

> **B3: D.** One implementation note grounded in the actual code: the post-validator (A) must do **tolerance-based numeric matching, not exact string containment** — this repo already renders floats rounded (`walk_forward.py`, `cli.py`, `benchmark.py` all format to 2–6 decimals), so a true card will *never* literal-match a raw `ScorePacket` float like `0.142876581`. Build `NumericAllowlist` as two typed buckets: (1) **float performance numbers** (returns, Sharpe, confidence, vol) matched within a rounding epsilon (e.g. round both card value and packet value to the same displayed precision before comparing), and (2) **structural ints** (`rank`, `universe_size`, `ranked_count`, `window_sessions`, `overlapping_sessions`, `price_rows_used`) matched exactly, no tolerance. Get the epsilon wrong in either direction and the validator either rejects every honest card (too strict) or stops catching the planted-false-Sharpe fixture test (too loose) — pin the epsilon value in the golden tests themselves, not just in the validator code.

Then continue to B4.

## Claude

I'll research the actual sources before answering — this is a real technical decision, not something to pattern-match from memory. I can't reach the Windows local file path (`D:\Users\_Anant\...`) from this WSL session, but the vault already has a Kronos deep-dive note I can read instead.

## Why TradingAgents is popular — and why that's a different question from "should we copy its topology"

Popularity here comes from three things that aren't the same as "the architecture is correct for a real trading desk": (1) it's the first well-packaged open-source repo (Apache-2.0, TauricResearch) applying the 2024 multi-agent-LLM wave to something everyone finds viscerally interesting — trading; (2) it mirrors real trading-firm org charts (analysts → researchers → traders → risk → PM), which reads as legitimate structure even before you check whether the structure earns its keep; (3) it reports "notable improvements in cumulative returns, Sharpe ratio, and max drawdown" versus baselines — a headline result, in a paper, with no mention (in what I could retrieve) of out-of-sample degradation, walk-forward testing, or a deflated-Sharpe-style multiple-testing correction. That last point matters more than it sounds: your own codebase (`gates/deflated_sharpe.py`) exists specifically because Bailey & López de Prado showed that an unadjusted good-looking backtest is frequently a multiple-testing artifact — and a framework with 4 analyst agents, bull/bear debate rounds, 3 risk personas, and a PM is a much larger implicit configuration space than a single quant strategy. By your own project's standard, that architecture's claimed edge is *more* suspect, not less, until it passes the same four gates your `[REDACTED]` had to pass.

## The actual architecture (from the paper + repo, not a summary of a summary)

```
Analyst Team (4 parallel LLM agents): Fundamentals | Sentiment (news/StockTwits/Reddit) | News (macro/FRED) | Technical (MACD/RSI)
        ↓
Researcher Team: Bull agent vs Bear agent — configurable N rounds of adversarial debate
        ↓
Trader Agent — synthesizes debate + analyst reports into a proposed action
        ↓
Risk Management Team — multiple risk-persona agents (aggressive/neutral/conservative) evaluate the trade
        ↓
Portfolio Manager — approves or rejects the transaction
```

Orchestration is **LangGraph** (graph-based state propagation between nodes, with checkpoint/resume). Data sources: Yahoo Finance, Alpha Vantage, news/sentiment feeds, FRED, and — notably — **Polymarket data as a sentiment input**, not as a separate tradeable asset class. That's a genuinely different use of prediction-market data than the vertical-2 question you already parked; worth knowing it exists but it doesn't reopen that decision.

Two facts from the repo itself matter more than the architecture diagram:

1. **"TradingAgents framework is designed for research purposes... It is not intended as financial, investment, or trading advice"** — and separately, the docs state results "are not guaranteed to match any published figure" due to LLM sampling variance and live data drift. The framework's own maintainers don't claim it's reliable enough for real capital.
2. **The Trader and Portfolio Manager agents are LLMs that make and approve the actual trading decision.** There is no deterministic gate underneath them — the debate's outcome *is* the decision.

## Why point 2 is the actual problem, not a stylistic quibble

Your project's single hardest-defended invariant — the one line repeated verbatim across `CLAUDE.md`, `YEAR_AHEAD_BASE.md` (`brain/models.py`'s `validate_human_identity`), and the original session charter — is: **Python owns facts/factors/gates/paper fills. AI proposes/explains from evidence only. AI must never approve or promote.** Your own `brain/models.py:76` literally hard-codes `_NON_HUMAN_IDENTITIES = {"ai", "agent", "bot", "claude", "cursor", "fable", "llm", "model", "system"}` and raises `ValueError` if any of those tries to approve a `PromotionDecision`. TradingAgents' Portfolio Manager role is precisely that forbidden thing: an LLM approving a transaction. Importing "bull/bear/trader" isn't a cosmetic addition of roles — it's importing the one decision topology your architecture was built, tested, and documented specifically to exclude. This isn't me being cautious for its own sake; it's the exact reason `test_package_invariants.py`-style hard checks exist in this codebase — to make certain categories of drift fail loudly instead of sliding in.

And it's worth naming plainly: this is the same shape of thing that came up with the V1/PM-vertical question and the UI question two turns ago. You wrote, at the very top of this Cursor session, in your own words: *"Do not copy multi-agent trade debate as edge"* and rated TradingAgents-scale swarm as *"default too much."* Wanting B+D+E now is a real reversal of your own stated rule, made three questions later, likely because seeing the actual paper made the pattern feel more credible than it did in the abstract. That's a legitimate thing to reconsider — but it should be a conscious supersede with a reason, not something that accretes silently across a few messages, which is exactly the "four generations of drift" failure mode your own postmortem was written to stop.

## Is B + D + E "possible"? Yes, mechanically. Here's what you'd actually be signing up for

- Wiring LangGraph-style debate + risk personas + a PM-approval agent into `brain/loop.py` means either (a) breaking `validate_human_identity`'s guarantee, or (b) building an elaborate LLM debate whose output then still has to pass through the human-approved `PromotionDecision` anyway — at which point the debate is theater that costs 10-15+ LLM calls per symbol per day and adds zero decision authority, because the gates and `anant` still decide. Cursor's own C1 table already named this: *"invents edge narrative."*
- Tutor (D) is genuinely cheap and safe to add, for a specific reason worth stating explicitly: **a Tutor pass sits downstream of a finalized `EvidenceCard` and never touches confidence, gates, or promote/demote.** It's a pure explanation/rendering layer. This is the one piece of your B+D+E ask that doesn't conflict with anything.
- A "swarm doing research on how to improve the codebase/strategies" — the thing you floated as "the first task for the brain" — is actually a *different, legitimate* use of the swarm pattern than bull/bear/trader. A fleet of agents reading arXiv/vault/OpenBB and proposing new `Citation` rows or draft `StrategySpec`s (still `PROPOSED`, still requiring `anant`'s approval, still gated by the same four tests before anything is `demo_eligible`) doesn't touch the decision path at all — it just feeds the citation layer that already exists. This is worth building, but as its own explicitly-scoped V1.1/V2 item (a research-proposer swarm), not folded into C1's analyst/critic topology question.

OpenBB, for the record, isn't architecturally relevant here — it's a data-consolidation/MCP layer for pulling in third-party sources, not an agent-orchestration reference. Nothing in it changes this calculus.

## My recommendation

**C1: keep B.** One Analyst, one Critic, no bull/bear/trader/PM debate — because that topology's decision-making role is the one thing this project's architecture is built to forbid, not because "simpler is safer" as a vague instinct. Add two things as separate, explicitly-scoped items, not folded into C1:

1. **Tutor** — a beginner-explanation pass over the finished `EvidenceCard`, decision-inert, safe to build in V1 or V1.1 your choice.
2. **Research-proposer swarm** — parallel agents reading external sources (papers, vault, OpenBB data) to draft `Citation`s and `PROPOSED` `StrategySpec`s for the brain, feeding the *existing* citation → human-approve → gates loop rather than replacing any part of it. This is the closest honest version of "the brain researches how to improve itself," and it's compatible with everything already locked. Scope it as its own future block (maybe C1a or a V1.1 item), not this decision.

Suggested reply to Cursor:

> **C1: B**, unchanged. Looked at TradingAgents (arXiv 2412.20138, TauricResearch repo) in depth — its Trader/Portfolio-Manager roles are LLMs that approve the trade directly, which is the one thing `brain/models.py`'s `validate_human_identity` (and the project's core law) exists to forbid; importing that topology isn't "more roles," it's handing promote/demote authority to a debate instead of the four gates + `anant`. Declining to reopen the "no multi-agent debate as edge" rule from the session charter.
>

# Guardrails (grep-first)

Before finishing any PR that touches user-facing strings or data paths:

1. No `BUY`/`SELL`/"guaranteed"/"risk-free" in user-facing output (whitelist enforcement code in `benchmark.py` / tests that assert the ban).
2. Action fields only: WATCH | HOLD | ACCUMULATE | REDUCE | AVOID | INSUFFICIENT_DATA.
3. Never fabricate missing prices/fundamentals — surface INSUFFICIENT_DATA / MISSING.
4. Confidence capped by QualityStatus precedence in `quality.py`.
5. No secrets in source/fixtures; `.env` gitignored.
6. No LLM calls in ingestion path modules.
7. No broker SDKs; no options/futures/crypto/margin/intraday paths this phase.

Use the `guardrail-auditor` agent (or `.claude/skills/guardrail-check`) before merge.
  ```

## Claude

I looked at what LangGraph actually solves and cross-checked it against this repo's own dependency practice before answering — the case against it is concrete, not just "keep it simple."

## What LangGraph is actually for

LangGraph is built for **dynamic, LLM-decided control flow**: nodes that can loop, conditional edges where the *model* chooses the next step, checkpointed state so a multi-hour/multi-turn agent workflow can resume after a crash, and shared graph state across many independently-authored agent nodes. Its value shows up when you have something like TradingAgents' bull/bear debate — an unknown number of rounds, agents whose outputs determine whether the graph loops again or exits.

Your V1 runner, per C2's own framing, is `assemble → analyst (one LLM call) → validate (deterministic Python) → critic (one LLM call) → write`. That's a fixed-length, linear pipeline with exactly two LLM call sites and no branching decided by a model. There's nothing for a graph engine to route, loop, or checkpoint — every one of LangGraph's actual selling points is solving a problem this pipeline doesn't have, because C1 already ruled out the thing (multi-round debate, dynamic agent-decided flow) that would create that problem.

## Three concrete reasons not to adopt it here

1. **It would reopen C1 by the back door.** LangGraph's whole design premise is "let the model influence what happens next." You just spent C1 establishing that gates and `anant` decide outcomes, not model output. Using a model-flow-control framework for a pipeline that explicitly has no model-controlled flow is importing the shape of the thing you rejected, even with the content stripped out.
2. **It conflicts with your own established dependency discipline, not just a vague "minimalism" preference.** I checked `pyproject.toml`: this project's entire dependency list is `pydantic`, `duckdb`, `typer`, `tomli`, `hypothesis`, `pytest`, `exchange_calendars` — seven packages, all boring, all stable, none from a framework with a fast-moving API surface. `YEAR_AHEAD_BASE.md`'s stated goal is a base "still correct a year from now." The LangChain/LangGraph family is well known for breaking changes across minor versions; pinning your two-role pipeline to it trades real future maintenance risk for zero present capability gain.
3. **Cost without payoff, precisely.** Every LangGraph concept you'd have to model — nodes, edges, a state schema, a compiled graph object — has to exist for *two sequential function calls*. That's not "using the right tool for the job," that's translation overhead with no corresponding flexibility you'll use.

## Where your instinct about libraries is right — just aimed at the wrong one

You're correct that this layer is under-using the ecosystem, but the actual gaps aren't orchestration, they're **structured-output enforcement** and **provider abstraction** — both of which C2/C3 already need and neither of which pulls in framework churn:

- **`instructor` or `pydantic-ai`** — thin libraries that force an LLM call to return an object validated directly against your existing `EvidenceCard`/`CriticReview` Pydantic models, retrying automatically on schema mismatch. This is a direct, mechanical assist to B3's numeric tripwire: constrain the shape the model can emit at the API boundary instead of relying solely on post-hoc regex/allowlist validation of free text.
- **`litellm`** — a thin, provider-agnostic call interface (Anthropic/OpenAI/Ollama behind one function signature). This is the natural implementation for C3's "pluggable provider interface with offline default" question, coming up next — write your own fixture provider under it the same way `csv_fixture` already works for prices.

Both are single-purpose, small, and don't touch control flow — they slot into option A/E's plain-function runner exactly as written, no redesign needed.

## Recommendation

**C2: E, unchanged. No LangGraph.** Fold the library note in as implementation guidance, not a new open question.

Suggested reply to Cursor:

> **C2: E.** No LangGraph — evaluated it directly against this pipeline's actual shape (fixed two-call linear sequence, no model-decided branching) and it solves problems C1 already ruled out (dynamic/looping agent flow). It would also be the first dependency in this project pulling from a framework family known for breaking API changes, against a stated "durable base" goal — cost with no corresponding capability need.
>
> Implementation note for the runner (`src/research_data/agents/runner.py`, path confirmed at F1): use **`instructor` or `pydantic-ai`** to bind the Analyst/Critic LLM calls directly to `EvidenceCard`/`CriticReview` schemas — validates shape at the API boundary and gives B3's tripwire less free text to police. Use **`litellm`** as the provider call interface for C3's pluggable-provider requirement, with a hand-written fixture provider for offline CI, mirroring the existing `csv_fixture` pattern.

## Claude

## Does litellm itself cost anything?

No. Confirmed directly from the repo and docs: `pip install litellm` is the open-source SDK — it's a translation/routing layer, not a billed service. When you call `completion(model="gemini/gemini-2.5-flash", ...)`, litellm forwards the request to whichever provider you named and you pay **that provider's** rate (which can be $0 if the provider itself is free). The **LiteLLM Proxy/Gateway** and **LiteLLM Enterprise** products you saw on the website are a separate, optional, self-hosted piece (virtual keys, spend tracking, admin dashboard, SSO) aimed at teams running many users through one gatekeeper — not something a two-call-per-symbol solo desk needs. C2/C3's plan (import the SDK directly into `runner.py`, no gateway) is the right shape; nothing changes there.

## The actual free-provider landscape, checked against current docs — not memory

| Provider | Free tier today | Structured/JSON output | Verdict for this project |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ollama** (local) | $0 forever, no rate limit, no network call at all | Depends on model; smaller local models are less reliable at strict schema adherence | Good as an *optional* fully-offline dev mode, not the one documented live default — hardware-dependent, not portable to whoever else runs the live smoke test |
| **Google Gemini (Flash)** | 1,500 requests/day, 10 RPM, 250K TPM, no card required | Native JSON mode / JSON schema on the free tier | **Best fit** — trivially covers your actual call volume, decent quality, zero setup friction |
| **Groq** | 30 RPM, 6–8K TPM, 1,000 requests/day, no card | JSON mode + tool calling on **all** models, fast inference | Strong alternate — open-weight models (Llama 3.3 70B, GPT-OSS-120B), good for disciplined extraction tasks |
| **GitHub Models** | 50 req/day (high-tier), 8K-in/4K-out caps | Yes, OpenAI-compatible | **Ruled out** — GitHub is retiring this service **July 30, 2026**, three weeks from today. Don't build a documented default on something already scheduled to disappear. |
| **Azure OpenAI ($100 credit)** | Not really "free tier" — pay-as-you-go against your credit | Full structured outputs | See below |

**Azure explains itself, and it's not a fluke.** Microsoft's own docs confirm: a new Azure OpenAI subscription starts every model deployment at **0 TPM quota** until you explicitly file a quota-increase request (`aka.ms/oai/stuquotarequest`), specifying subscription/region/model/desired throughput, with propagation taking up to 15 minutes *after approval* — approval itself is not instant and can take longer depending on region/model demand. That's exactly the "tokens rejected very quickly" experience you described — it's Azure's default gatekeeping posture, not something you did wrong, and it's real ramp-up time before you get a usable deployment.

## The reality check that actually matters here

Your V1 scope (per B1–C2, already locked) is: fixture-only CI, plus **one live smoke card** for A3 — an analyst call and a critic call, maybe repeated a few dozen times total while you're debugging the golden tests. That is a trivial volume against *any* of these tiers; Gemini's 1,500/day or Groq's 1,000/day free ceilings aren't remotely close to being tested by this project's actual usage. Chasing "genuinely $0" is solving a cost problem you don't have yet — the real risk isn't dollars, it's the two things free tiers are actually known for: **weaker models** and **rate-limit-induced delays**, which is precisely what you said you want to avoid. The good news is B1–B4's own design works in your favor here: the Analyst/Critic's job under your locked rules isn't creative reasoning, it's **disciplined, literal quoting from a fixed allowlist inside a rigid schema** (B3/B4). That's a task free-tier and mid-size open models are genuinely good at — you're not asking Gemini Flash or Llama 3.3 70B to out-reason GPT-5, you're asking it to not invent a number, which your validator (B3) checks anyway regardless of which model produced the draft.

**Recommendation: Gemini Flash as the documented live default, Groq as the noted alternate, Azure held in reserve.**

- Primary: `gemini/gemini-<current-flash-alias>` — $0, no card, native JSON schema, 1,500 req/day, essentially zero setup friction. (Confirm the exact current model string in Google's docs at implementation time — these aliases get renamed/versioned.)
- One thing worth being explicit about rather than silent on: Google's free tier terms permit using free-tier prompts for model training. For this project that's public market data, not credentials or PII, so the exposure is low — but note it in the `.env.example` comment so it's a conscious choice, not an assumption. If that's ever a concern, Groq is the documented fallback (worth confirming Groq's own data-use terms before finalizing — I didn't get an explicit answer on that from what I pulled).
- Azure's $100 credit: keep it as a reserved paid option for later, higher-volume work — e.g., if the research-proposer swarm (parked under C1) ever ships and needs real throughput. Spending the quota-request ramp-up time now, for a call volume this small, is exactly the kind of delay you said you want to avoid.

Suggested reply to Cursor:

> **C3: E.** Confirmed litellm's SDK is free/open-source (you only pay the underlying provider — the paid Proxy/Gateway/Enterprise tiers are a separate, unneeded product for this scope). Researched current free-tier terms directly: **Gemini Flash** is the documented live default (`LLM_MODEL=gemini/<current-flash-alias>` in `.env.example`, no key committed) — 1,500 req/day free, no card, native JSON schema, comfortably covers V1's actual volume (fixture CI + one A3 live smoke card). Note in `.env.example`: Google's free tier may use prompts for training; low-stakes for public market data but stated explicitly. **Groq** documented as the alternate (`groq/llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, JSON mode + tool calling on all models, 1,000 req/day free) if that matters. **GitHub Models excluded** — retiring 2026-07-30. **Azure OpenAI credit** held in reserve for later higher-volume work, not the V1 path — new subscriptions default to 0 TPM quota requiring an explicit increase request with real turnaround time, not worth the ramp-up for this call volume.

## Claude

Before answering, I want to flag something: this message's worry (rate limits, backup providers, "running almost all the time") doesn't actually match what C4 is asking, and it also doesn't match what's been locked so far. Let me check one thing — whether litellm has a built-in fallback mechanism — since that's the real answer to the rate-limit concern, separate from C4 entirely.

Two things to untangle here — C4 doesn't actually have anything to do with rate limits, and "running almost all the time hunting for information" describes something bigger than what's been locked so far. Let me handle both.

## C4 is a different question than the one you're worried about

C4 is asking **where in the codebase an LLM import is legally allowed to appear** — a static/lint boundary, not a runtime behavior. Its whole point is making sure `litellm` (or `openai`/`anthropic`/etc.) can never get imported into `gates/`, `factors/`, `brain/`, or `paper/engine.py`, so the deterministic math that decides promote/demote can never accidentally depend on a network call. Rate limits, provider count, and uptime are irrelevant to this specific question — a strict import boundary is exactly as easy to enforce whether you're calling one provider or five. Cursor's own reasoning here is sound and doesn't need adjustment for your rate-limit concern.

**C4: E**, unchanged. `test_security_scope.py` (or a sibling) should fail the build if `litellm|openai|anthropic|instructor|pydantic_ai|google.generativeai` appears anywhere under `src/research_data/` except `agents/`, with `agents/llm_client.py` as the one file that actually imports `litellm`. Everything else (`runner.py`, prompts, CLI) calls through that seam.

## Where your rate-limit worry actually belongs — and it's already solved

I checked: **litellm ships a `Router` in the open-source SDK** (not gateway-only) that does exactly what you're asking for — an ordered list of provider deployments with automatic fallback on 429s/timeouts/errors, cooldown tracking, and retries. Config is a plain list:

```python
router = Router(model_list=[
    {"model_name": "analyst", "litellm_params": {"model": "gemini/gemini-2.5-flash"}},
    {"model_name": "analyst", "litellm_params": {"model": "groq/llama-3.3-70b-versatile"}},
    {"model_name": "analyst", "litellm_params": {"model": "ollama/llama3.1"}},  # local, zero network dependency
], num_retries=3)
```

That gives you Gemini → Groq → local Ollama as an automatic chain, entirely inside `agents/llm_client.py` — the single seam C4 already locks down. This doesn't change C4's answer at all; it's an implementation detail that lives in the one file the boundary already permits. And it's the *correct-sized* answer to "don't want to be limited by rate limits" for what's actually been scoped: fixture-only CI plus one live smoke card. That volume will never come close to tripping even Gemini's free tier alone, let alone a 3-provider fallback chain.

## But "running almost all the time hunting for information" is not that

That phrase describes a **continuously-running background agent** — something polling/fetching external sources on an ongoing basis. Nothing locked so far is that. A1/A3 scoped exactly two LLM call sites (one analyst card, one critic review), on demand, not a daemon. The only thing in this whole decision log that's even adjacent to "hunting for information continuously" is the **research-proposer swarm you and I explicitly parked at C1** — reading external research to draft citations/specs — and even that was scoped as occasional, human-triggered, not an always-on process. A genuinely continuous background loop would burn through even generous free tiers in hours, not days, and needs its own real conversation: a budget, a schedule, a citation-ingest contract (which is literally D1, next) — not something to fold into C4's answer by implication.

## Claude

I checked `brain/store.py` directly before answering — Cursor's stated "dup risk" for option A isn't hypothetical, it's exactly how the code behaves today, and that same fact reveals a real correction needed in option B.

## What the code actually does right now

`Citation.citation_id` defaults to `_new_id()` — a random `uuid.uuid4()` — and `BrainStore` has **only** `add_citation`, `get_citation`, `list_citations`. There is no `update_citation`, no dedup check, and `run_quality_momentum_study.py` confirms the exact pattern Cursor described: it hand-builds `Citation(retrieved_at=now, **entry)` for each literature source and calls `brain.add_citation()` — every run of that script would insert brand-new duplicate rows for the same paper, because nothing computes a stable id from the content. So the dup-risk problem is real and current, not speculative.

## The one correction to make before locking B

Cursor's B says: *"hash(vault_relpath + mtime or content hash) → stable citation_id... Re-ingest updates if hash changes."* That phrasing implies overwriting the same row in place when a note's content changes. That's wrong for this store, for two reasons:

1. **There's no `update_citation` method to overwrite with** — you'd have to add one, which is unnecessary work.
2. **It would silently corrupt historical evidence.** `PromotionDecision.evidence_citation_ids` records which citations justified a past promote/demote decision. If editing a vault note's claims mutates the *same* `citation_id` in place, a decision made last month now points at a citation whose content has quietly changed since — the audit trail lies about what was actually cited at decision time. `brain/models.py`'s own docstring is explicit: *"Every model here is an auditable fact."* An in-place-mutable citation isn't one.

**The correct design:** hash `vault_relpath + content-hash-of-the-claims-section` (not mtime — mtime changes on a touch with no content change, which would wrongly mint a new id for a no-op edit). Same path + unchanged claims → same id → re-ingest is a true no-op (`get_citation` finds it, skip insert). Edited claims → different hash → a **new** citation row, old one left untouched and still validly referenced by anything that already cited it. This needs zero new store methods — `add_citation` as it exists today is sufficient; the ingest CLI just checks `get_citation(computed_id)` before deciding whether to insert.

`D` (journal auto-link) doesn't have this problem and needs no change: a `JournalEntry` is written once and never mutated, so `citation_id = hash("journal_lesson:" + journal_entry_id)` is safe as a stable key with no content-drift concern.

`A` (manual CLI) has a softer, human-error version of the same risk: nothing stops re-running `cite-add` for "Jegadeesh & Titman 1993" twice with two different UUIDs. Not worth a hard block (legitimate multi-edition papers exist), but `cite-add` should do a normalized title/author check against `list_citations()` and warn — not silently duplicate the literature citation graph.

## The optional sub-question: claims required non-empty?

**Allow empty at ingest, require non-empty at use.** `Citation.claims` has no length validator today, and it shouldn't gain one at the model level — a vault note tracked via `cite-from-vault` before its author has written a `## Claims` section is still a legitimate thing to have in the store (you want the source tracked as soon as you start reading it, not blocked until you've finished summarizing it). The fail-closed check belongs at the point a citation is actually *used* — whatever function assembles `evidence_citation_ids` for a `PROPOSED StrategySpec` or a `PromotionDecision` should reject any citation with `claims == []` at that point, not at ingest. This mirrors the architecture's existing separation between "a record can exist" (`PROPOSED` specs exist before approval) and "a record can be acted on" (approval requires a human identity) — same pattern, applied to citations.

## Recommendation

**D1: E**, with the content-hash correction to B folded in as an implementation note, not a reopen.

Path+content unchanged → same id → re-ingest is a no-op (checked via `get_citation` before insert). Content changed → new id, new row; the old citation stays untouched so any `PromotionDecision.evidence_citation_ids` that already cited it keeps pointing at what was actually cited at decision time (mtime-based hashing would either false-trigger on a no-op touch or, if implemented as an in-place update, silently mutate evidence behind a past decision — violates `brain/models.py`'s "every model here is an auditable fact").

## D2 — StrategySpec proposal contract (human V1 / LLM V1.1)

**Why it matters:** Proposer is deferred, but **humans already insert PROPOSED→APPROVED specs** (study runner). Locking the contract now prevents V1.1 from inventing a second shape and clarifies what V1’s critic must *not* emit (no param drafts). Bailey/LdP: every new tried configuration that enters the gate harness increments effective `n_trials` for DSR — proposals that spam param grids poison gate 4.

Today’s `StrategySpec`: `name`, `version`, `description`, `proposed_by`, `citation_ids`, `factor_dependencies`, `params`, optional `hook_ref`, status/promotion fields.

| | Option | Required beyond today | Hook / params policy | `n_trials` honesty |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Status quo — any dict `params`, optional `hook_ref` | None | Loose | DSR under-counts if many silent trials |
| **B** | Require non-empty `citation_ids` with non-empty claims at propose-time; `hook_ref` must resolve via `resolve_hook` before approve | Cite discipline | Existing hooks only until new code lands | Soft |
| **C** | **Param-delta only** vs a parent `spec_id` (`params_delta`, `parent_spec_id`); no new `hook_ref` in V1/V1.1 without a code PR | Thin proposals | New hooks = engineering, not AI | Each approved+tested version counts |
| **D** | **B + C** + explicit `declared_n_trials` or auto-count prior `brain_test_runs` distinct specs sharing hook family | Full contract | Param deltas preferred | Feeds DSR correctly |
| **E** | Allow freeform new hook_ref strings pointing at nonexistent modules | Fast ideation | Fail at approve/runtime | Messy |

**Recommended default: D** as the **standing contract** for any PROPOSED spec (enforce on human propose CLI in V1 Cursor prereqs; LLM proposer inherits in V1.1). V1 AI still does **not** emit StrategySpecs (A1/A2) — only CriticReview. New strategy *code* remains a normal PR, not a citation.

## Claude

I checked the actual `n_trials` mechanism and `resolve_hook` before answering — Cursor's D option has one real statistical error worth catching before it locks in, plus a definitive answer to the optional sub-question.

## `n_trials` is already auto-counted today — and Cursor's "hook family" framing would make it wrong

`BrainStore.count_tested_specs()` already exists: `SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT spec_id) FROM brain_test_runs`. Its own docstring states the principle precisely: *"every strategy configuration that reached testing counts as a selection-bias trial."* This is a **global** count across every spec ever gate-tested — not scoped to a hook, a family, or anything else.

Cursor's D option proposes "auto-count prior `brain_test_runs` distinct specs **sharing hook family**." That's a real mistake, not a style choice: Bailey & López de Prado's deflation term exists because *whichever configuration you ended up promoting was implicitly selected as the best-of-everything-you-tried* — and "everything you tried" means every spec that ever went through the gate harness, regardless of which hook or strategy family it belongs to. If you eventually have 5 momentum variants and 3 mean-reversion variants and promote the best of the 8, restricting the trial count to "specs sharing this hook family" would report `n_trials=5` (or fewer) instead of 8 — understating the selection bias exactly as the strategy pack grows, which is the opposite of what DSR is for. **Don't add hook-family scoping. Keep `count_tested_specs()`'s existing global count exactly as it is.**

## The optional sub-question has a clear answer: reject `declared_n_trials` entirely, not just "prefer" the auto-count

This isn't a close call. `Docs/[REDACTED].md`'s own risk register already names this exact failure mode — *"DSR self-deception via trial accounting"* — and its mitigation is explicitly: *"trial count grows naturally as specs accumulate"*, i.e., derived from what's actually recorded, never typed in. A `declared_n_trials` field on a `PROPOSED` spec (before any gate has run) would be the **first place in this entire codebase where a number entering gate math comes from free text instead of being derived from stored facts** — precisely the category of thing `quality.py`'s confidence-cap precedence, `evidence.py`'s provenance requirements, and every guardrail in `CLAUDE.md` exist to prevent. There's no version of "declare it now, but honestly" that's worth the exposure once an LLM proposer exists in V1.1 and could write down whatever number makes gate 4 pass. Auto-count from `count_tested_specs()` only — full stop, no override field on the model at all.

## `resolve_hook` confirms C is right, and tightens B slightly

`brain/loop.py:resolve_hook` partitions `hook_ref` on `":"`, imports the module, and raises `BrainLoopError` if it doesn't resolve to a callable. This confirms option E fails exactly as Cursor described (loud, but late) and confirms C's constraint is real, not a policy preference: **a new `hook_ref` cannot exist without someone first writing and merging the Python function** — there's no code path where a hook can be conjured by proposal alone. Good, no correction needed there.

One tightening to B: check `resolve_hook` succeeds **at propose-time**, not just "before approve." There's no cost to failing earlier, and every other fail-closed check in this codebase (`INSUFFICIENT_DATA`, quality caps, citation/claims-at-use) fires as early as possible rather than at the last safe moment. A param-delta proposal should always be reusing an existing, already-resolvable hook (per C) — so there's no legitimate case where hook resolution needs to wait until approval.

## Implementation note on the new fields

`params_delta` and `parent_spec_id` don't exist on `StrategySpec` today — this is a real schema addition, not just a policy statement, so it belongs on Cursor's prereq list. Recommend: `params` stays the **fully-resolved, self-contained** dict (computed once at propose-time by merging the parent's `params` with `params_delta`), while `params_delta` + `parent_spec_id` are kept purely for provenance/readability. That way `resolve_hook` + gate execution never needs to walk a parent chain at runtime — it just reads `params` like it always has — and a human reviewing the proposal can still see "what changed" at a glance via `params_delta`.

## Recommendation

**D2: D**, with the correction folded in.

Scoping to a hook family would under-count trials exactly as the strategy pack grows, which is the opposite of what DSR needs.

=== deflated_sharpe.py full ===
"""Gate 4 — deflated Sharpe ratio (Bailey & López de Prado 2014, SSRN 2460551).

The best backtest among N tried configurations has an inflated Sharpe. The
DSR asks: given how many trials were run (from brain test-run records) and
the non-normality of the returns, what is the probability that the true
Sharpe exceeds the expected maximum Sharpe of N skill-less trials?

    DSR = Phi( ((SR_hat - SR0) * sqrt(T - 1)) /
               sqrt(1 - g3*SR_hat + ((g4 - 1)/4) * SR_hat^2) )

with SR_hat the per-period Sharpe, T the number of returns, g3 skewness,
g4 (non-excess) kurtosis, and SR0 the expected max trial Sharpe:

    SR0 = sqrt(V[SR_trials]) * ((1-γ) * Z^-1(1 - 1/N) + γ * Z^-1(1 - 1/(N·e)))

γ = Euler-Mascheroni. With one trial (or no trial-Sharpe variance) SR0 = 0
and the DSR reduces to the probabilistic Sharpe ratio against 0.

Default pass rule: DSR >= 0.95.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
import statistics as stats_mod
from dataclasses import dataclass

from research_data.gates.metrics import (
    DEFAULT_COST_BPS_PER_SIDE,
    GateResult,
    StrategyReturns,
    kurtosis,
    mean_std,
    skewness,
)

EULER_MASCHERONI = 0.5772156649015329
_NORMAL = stats_mod.NormalDist()


def expected_max_sharpe(n_trials: int, variance_trial_sharpes: float) -> float:
    """E[max SR] across n skill-less trials with the given SR variance."""
    if n_trials <= 1 or variance_trial_sharpes <= 0:
        return 0.0
    z1 = _NORMAL.inv_cdf(1.0 - 1.0 / n_trials)
    z2 = _NORMAL.inv_cdf(1.0 - 1.0 / (n_trials * math.e))
    return math.sqrt(variance_trial_sharpes) * (
        (1.0 - EULER_MASCHERONI) * z1 + EULER_MASCHERONI * z2
    )


def deflated_sharpe_probability(
    returns: list[float],
    n_trials: int,
    trial_sharpes: list[float] | None = None,
) -> tuple[float | None, dict]:
    """DSR probability and its intermediate values (for the audit trail).

    ``trial_sharpes`` are per-period Sharpes of other tried configurations
    (from brain test runs); their variance drives the deflation term. Without
    at least two, SR0 falls back to 0 (pure PSR — no selection-bias credit is
    *added*, none is forgiven).
    """
    t = len(returns)
    if t < 3:
        return None, {"reason": f"only {t} returns; need >= 3"}
    mean, std = mean_std(returns)
    if std == 0:
        return None, {"reason": "returns have no variance; Sharpe undefined"}
    sr_hat = mean / std
    g3 = skewness(returns)
    g4 = kurtosis(returns)

    variance_trials = 0.0
    if trial_sharpes is not None and len(trial_sharpes) >= 2:
        variance_trials = stats_mod.pvariance(trial_sharpes)
    sr0 = expected_max_sharpe(n_trials, variance_trials)

    denominator_sq = 1.0 - g3 * sr_hat + ((g4 - 1.0) / 4.0) * sr_hat**2
    if denominator_sq <= 0:
        return None, {
            "reason": "non-normality adjustment is degenerate for this series",
            "sr_hat": sr_hat,
            "skewness": g3,
            "kurtosis": g4,
        }
    z = (sr_hat - sr0) * math.sqrt(t - 1) / math.sqrt(denominator_sq)
    probability = _NORMAL.cdf(z)
    return probability, {
        "sr_hat_per_period": sr_hat,
        "sr0_expected_max": sr0,
        "n_trials": n_trials,
        "variance_trial_sharpes": variance_trials,
        "skewness": g3,
        "kurtosis": g4,
        "t_periods": t,
        "z_statistic": z,
    }


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DeflatedSharpeParams:
    min_probability: float = 0.95
    cost_bps_per_side: float = DEFAULT_COST_BPS_PER_SIDE


def run_deflated_sharpe_gate(
    strategy: StrategyReturns,
    n_trials: int,
    trial_sharpes: list[float] | None = None,
    params: DeflatedSharpeParams = DeflatedSharpeParams(),
) -> GateResult:
    """The DSR probability must clear ``min_probability`` (default 0.95)."""
    net = strategy.net_returns(params.cost_bps_per_side)
    probability, details = deflated_sharpe_probability(net, n_trials, trial_sharpes)
    inputs = {
        "min_probability": params.min_probability,
        "cost_bps_per_side": params.cost_bps_per_side,
        "n_trials": n_trials,
        "n_trial_sharpes_provided": len(trial_sharpes or []),
    }
    if probability is None:
        return GateResult(
            gate="deflated_sharpe",
            passed=False,
            inputs=inputs,
            outputs=details,
            notes=[f"DSR undefined: {details.get('reason', 'unknown')}. Fails closed."],
        )
    passed = probability >= params.min_probability
    return GateResult(
        gate="deflated_sharpe",
        passed=passed,
        inputs=inputs,
        outputs={"deflated_sharpe_probability": probability, **details},
        notes=(
            []
            if passed
            else [
                f"DSR probability {probability:.4f} < required {params.min_probability}."
            ]
        ),
    )

        rows = self._conn.execute(
            "SELECT * FROM brain_test_runs WHERE gate_name = ? "
            "ORDER BY created_at, test_run_id",
            [gate_name.value],
        ).fetchall()
        return [_row_to_test_run(r) for r in rows]

    def count_tested_specs(self) -> int:
        """Number of distinct specs with at least one recorded gate run.

        This is the trial count fed to the deflated-Sharpe gate: every strategy
        configuration that reached testing counts as a selection-bias trial.
        """
        row = self._conn.execute(
            "SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT spec_id) FROM brain_test_runs"
        ).fetchone()
        return int(row[0]) if row else 0

## D3 — Forbidden proposal classes in V1 (and standing reject list)

**Why it matters:** Even with no LLM proposer in V1, CriticReview text and any future PROPOSED path must not smuggle changes that bypass the four gates, universe lock, or Kronos reserve. This is the reject list Fable/Cursor validators enforce (schema + grep + propose-time checks).

| | Option | Reject list scope |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | Soft prompt only (“don’t change gates”) | Unenforceable |
| **B** | Hard reject: gate constant edits (`504/126/126`, DSR≥0.95, cost bps, min_windows, …) | Necessary but incomplete |
| **C** | **B** + universe expansion / symbol add-remove via spec params + cost-model edits + execution language (`BUY`/`SELL`/guaranteed/risk-free) | Strong |
| **D** | **C** + Kronos promote influence / attaching Kronos packets + auto-promote language + loosening QualityStatus precedence | Full product-law reject list |
| **E** | Allow gate “sensitivity tweaks” as param deltas | **Reject** — literature defaults are settled law |

**Recommended default: D.** Propose-time / CriticReview validators reject any draft that: mutates gate literature defaults; expands the 14-symbol universe via params; edits cost bps; mentions Kronos as actionable evidence; uses execution language; claims AI-approved promotion. Critic **may** suggest human HOLD/DEMOTE citing gate whitelist numbers — that is not a gate-constant change.

**Bad choice breaks:**
- **A/E** → curve-fit via “just loosen OOS.”
- **B only** → universe/cost/Kronos backdoors remain.
- Allowing Kronos in critic input → violates reserved schema until RankIC session.

## Claude

I traced the actual gate-construction and hook-call code paths before answering — this reveals something better than what D3 assumes, but also a real fragility worth hardening.

## The good news: the "backdoors" D3 worries about don't exist today — but not because anything blocks them

`GateHarness.run_and_record()` is called in the study runner as `GateHarness().run_and_record(...)` — **zero constructor arguments**, meaning `GateHarnessConfig()` always resolves to its literature-default dataclasses (`OOSParams`, `MonteCarloParams`, `WalkForwardParams(step_sessions=126, min_windows=3, ...)`, `DeflatedSharpeParams(min_probability=0.95, cost_bps_per_side=5.0)`). Nothing in the current call chain ever constructs `GateHarnessConfig` from `spec.params`.

Same for universe: `quality_momentum_tilt_hook(params: dict, price_api, universe: list[str], start, end, ...)` takes `universe` as a **caller-supplied positional argument** — the study runner passes `universe = list(config.universe.symbols)` straight from `assets.toml`. The hook's `params` dict only carries strategy-level knobs (`momentum_weight`, `quality_weight`, `price_source`) — there's no code path where a spec's `params` could smuggle in a symbol list or a gate constant, because nothing ever reads those keys out of `params` in the first place.

That's genuinely reassuring, but notice *why* it holds: it's an accident of the current call graph, not an enforced invariant. Nothing today would catch a well-intentioned future refactor — say, Fable adding "flexibility" by having `GateHarness(config_from(spec.params))` — from quietly opening exactly the backdoor D3 is trying to close. A propose-time text validator (checking whether a `PROPOSED` spec's description/params *mentions* changing a gate constant) only catches a proposal that tries to talk its way through; it does nothing if a future engineer just wires the plumbing directly.

## The concrete fix: make it a structural test, not just a content validator

Add to the same test family as C4's `test_security_scope.py` (same idea: prove something is structurally impossible, not just currently avoided):

1. **`test_gate_constants_never_spec_sourced`** — assert `GateHarnessConfig(` and each of `OOSParams(`/`MonteCarloParams(`/`WalkForwardParams(`/`DeflatedSharpeParams(` are constructed with zero non-default arguments anywhere under `src/` and `scripts/` outside `tests/`. This protects the *literature defaults themselves*, not just proposal text.
2. **`test_hook_signatures_dont_read_universe_from_params`** — grep/AST check that no strategy hook function body indexes `params["universe"]`/`params.get("symbols")`/similar. `universe` must stay a caller-supplied argument, never something a spec smuggles through its own `params` dict.
3. Extend the same suite to assert `kronos_reserved` is never imported anywhere under `agents/` — `KronosForecastEvidence`'s own `validate_admission_gates` already makes constructing invalid Kronos evidence a `ValueError` (USABLE quality + RankIC ≥ 0.03 required), so nothing can build a *fake* forecast — but nothing today stops a future CriticReview prompt from being handed a real one prematurely. The import-boundary test is the actual backstop, matching C4's own pattern.

## One nuance on the execution-language check

`benchmark.py` already has `_EXECUTION_TOKENS = frozenset({"BUY", "SELL", "HOLD", "BUY NOW", "SELL NOW"})` — note it bans **"HOLD" too**, which is correct *for that module specifically* (a pure data-sanity reporter has no legitimate reason to say hold/buy/sell about anything). Don't reuse that exact frozenset verbatim for card/critic validation — `HOLD` is one of the six legal `ActionLabel` values there. The card/critic banned-token check should be `BUY`/`SELL`/"guaranteed"/"risk-free" (and close variants) only, with the `action` field itself validated against the `ActionLabel` enum as its own separate allow-list check, not folded into the same banned-word set `benchmark.py` uses.

## Recommendation

**D3: D**, with the structural-test hardening as the real implementation requirement, not optional polish.

Suggested reply to Cursor:

> **D3: D.** Traced the actual call paths: `GateHarness()` is invoked with zero config args today (`GateHarnessConfig()` always resolves to literature defaults), and strategy hooks take `universe` as a caller-supplied positional argument, never read from inside `params` — so the backdoors this question worries about don't exist *yet*, but only because nothing wires them, not because anything blocks them. That's fragile against a future refactor, not proposal text.
>
> Concrete requirement, not just propose-time/critic prose checks: add structural tests alongside `test_security_scope.py` — (1) `GateHarnessConfig`/`OOSParams`/`MonteCarloParams`/`WalkForwardParams`/`DeflatedSharpeParams` are never constructed with non-default arguments outside `tests/`; (2) no strategy hook reads `universe`/`symbols`/cost-bps keys out of its `params` dict; (3) `kronos_reserved` is never imported under `agents/`. These protect the literature defaults and universe lock structurally — a proposal can't argue its way around a wiring path that doesn't exist, whereas a future "helpful" refactor could easily create one if nothing tests against it.
>
> One correction: don't reuse `benchmark.py`'s `_EXECUTION_TOKENS` verbatim for card/critic validation — it bans `HOLD` too, which is correct for that pure data-reporter context but wrong here, since `HOLD` is a legal `ActionLabel`. Card/critic banned-token check = `BUY`/`SELL`/"guaranteed"/"risk-free" only; `action` field validated separately against the `ActionLabel` enum.

## D4 — Human gate UX

**Why it matters:** `BrainStore.approve_spec` / `reject_spec` / `record_gate_outcome_decision` already require human identity via `validate_human_identity`. There is **no** brain Typer surface today (`cli.py` = init-db / ingest / audit / benchmark; study script wires approve inline). CriticReview artifacts need a clear path: human reads card+review → runs existing decision APIs. Vault markdown PRs are nice for narrative, but DuckDB remains SoT for promote/demote.

| | Option | Approve / HOLD / DEMOTE path | CriticReview intake |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Extend Typer: `brain propose/approve/reject/decide` + `analyze-symbol` / `critique-spec` | CLI only; `--approver anant` | Write JSON under `data/cards/`; decide cites review ids in rationale |
| **B** | Vault markdown PR / note as the gate | Human edits vault; agent syncs DB | Easy to desync from DuckDB |
| **C** | Study-script only (status quo) | Works for pack; awkward for cards | No first-class AI desk UX |
| **D** | **A + vault mirror note** (optional export of card/review to TradingView folder; DB remains SoT) | CLI decides | Mirror for reading in Obsidian |
| **E** | Auto-apply CriticReview demotion if confidence below threshold | AI decides | **Forbidden** by law |

**Recommended default: A** (smallest durable). Add brain + analyze CLI commands; keep `--approver` validated; refuse non-human identities. Optional vault export can wait (F3) so V1 doesn’t depend on Obsidian for truth. If you want reading comfort without two SoTs, pick **D** with explicit “DB wins on conflict.”

**Bad choice breaks:**
- **B** → promote state in vault ≠ `is_demo_eligible`.
- **E** → deletes human gate.
- **C** → A3 live card has no desk command path.
- **D without “DB wins”** → dual SoT fights.

Reply **A / B / C / D / E**. Then **D5**.```. This is a decision making question that needs to be thought through based on what is written inside jarvis and the codebase build. Let's analyze our work before coming back with a finaluzed answer.

## Claude

I checked `cli.py`, the study script, and the store's approval methods directly before answering — there's a real inconsistency worth catching, and one place where I'd override Cursor's own sequencing call based on how you've actually been working this whole session.

## What's actually there today

`cli.py` has exactly four Typer commands: `init-db`, `ingest-prices`, `audit-prices`, `benchmark`. Zero brain-related commands exist. All current brain interaction — `get_or_register_spec`, `approve_spec(spec.spec_id, approved_by=approver)`, `record_decision(..., decided_by=args.approver)` — lives inline inside `scripts/run_quality_momentum_study.py`, invoked as `python scripts/run_quality_momentum_study.py --record-decision --approver anant`.

Two things worth noting precisely:

1. **The human gate is already enforced at the store layer, not the CLI layer.** `approve_spec`, `reject_spec`, and `retire_spec` each call `validate_human_identity(approved_by, ...)` themselves before touching the database. Adding a CLI wrapper is safe by construction — there's no new place for the guardrail to be bypassed, because the enforcement point already sits below wherever the UI lives. This confirms option A/D can't accidentally weaken anything; the CLI is pure convenience on top of a check that already exists.

2. **The study script uses `argparse` (`--approver`, `args.approver`), while `cli.py` uses Typer (`@app.command`).** These are two different CLI frameworks already coexisting in this repo. `YEAR_AHEAD_BASE.md`'s own module map treats them as different categories on purpose: `cli.py` is *"Typer CLI"* (the durable, installed, general-purpose desk surface), while `scripts/run_quality_momentum_study.py` is explicitly labeled *"manual live study"* — a one-off research-pass runner, not part of the installed package's UX. New `brain propose/approve/reject/decide` + `analyze-symbol`/`critique-spec` commands are exactly the kind of repeatable, general-purpose action that belongs in `cli.py`'s Typer app, not bolted onto the throwaway argparse script — otherwise you'd have three inconsistent CLI conventions in one repo instead of two. (Small correction for precision: the store method is `record_decision`, not `record_gate_outcome_decision`.)

Cursor's **A** is the right lettered answer — DB+CLI as the real gate, human identity enforced where it already lives. But its note defers the vault mirror (D's addition) to F3 as "smallest durable." I'd pull that forward into V1, and I can point to *why* directly from this session rather than guessing: every non-trivial decision in this whole questionnaire has been grounded by reading vault notes — Postmortem, Session Findings, AI Engine Architecture, Kronos deep-dive — because that's where you actually keep and revisit context. A card/review sitting only as a DuckDB row or a raw JSON file under `data/cards/` is invisible to that workflow; you'd have to leave Obsidian and query the database or open a file just to read something the desk already produced.

The reason this is safe to pull forward, and doesn't reopen B's actual danger: **D's vault export is one-directional and read-only** — DB → vault markdown, display only, nothing reads back. B was dangerous because it let a human *edit the vault note* and expected that edit to flow back as authoritative, creating a real dual-SoT fight (`Session Findings — Post Base`'s own lock is explicit: *"Desk-is-real bar... not UI"* — the vault was never meant to be where trust lives). D's mirror never touches that boundary; it's a pure function of an already-finalized `EvidenceCard`/`CriticReview`, exported for reading comfort. There's no new data model and no new decision logic — just a markdown-writer over data that's already validated. Given how cheap that is and how clearly this session shows your actual reading habit, deferring it to F3 mostly costs ergonomics for no real risk reduction.
