    def approve_spec(
        self,
        spec_id: str,
        approved_by: str,
        approved_at: datetime | None = None,
    ) -> StrategySpec:
        """Human gate: PROPOSED → APPROVED. ``approved_by`` must be a human."""
        approved_by = validate_human_identity(approved_by, "approved_by")
        spec = self.get_spec(spec_id)
        if spec.status != SpecStatus.PROPOSED:
            raise BrainStoreError(
                f"Only PROPOSED specs can be approved; {spec_id} is {spec.status.value}"
            )
        approved_at = approved_at or datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        self._conn.execute(
            """
            UPDATE brain_strategy_specs
            SET status = ?, approved_by = ?, approved_at = ?
            WHERE spec_id = ?
            """,
            [SpecStatus.APPROVED.value, approved_by, _to_db_ts(approved_at), spec_id],
        )
        return self.get_spec(spec_id)

    def reject_spec(self, spec_id: str, reason: str, decided_by: str) -> StrategySpec:
        """Human gate: PROPOSED → REJECTED with a stated reason."""
        decided_by = validate_human_identity(decided_by, "decided_by")
        if not reason.strip():
            raise BrainStoreError("rejection requires a non-empty reason")
        spec = self.get_spec(spec_id)
        if spec.status != SpecStatus.PROPOSED:
            raise BrainStoreError(
                f"Only PROPOSED specs can be rejected; {spec_id} is {spec.status.value}"
            )
        self._conn.execute(
            "UPDATE brain_strategy_specs SET status = ?, status_reason = ? WHERE spec_id = ?",
            [SpecStatus.REJECTED.value, reason, spec_id],
        )
        return self.get_spec(spec_id)

    def retire_spec(self, spec_id: str, reason: str, decided_by: str) -> StrategySpec:
        """Human gate: APPROVED → RETIRED (spec leaves active rotation)."""
        decided_by = validate_human_identity(decided_by, "decided_by")
        if not reason.strip():
            raise BrainStoreError("retiring requires a non-empty reason")
        spec = self.get_spec(spec_id)
        if spec.status != SpecStatus.APPROVED:
            raise BrainStoreError(
                f"Only APPROVED specs can be retired; {spec_id} is {spec.status.value}"
            )
        self._conn.execute(
            "UPDATE brain_strategy_specs SET status = ?, status_reason = ? WHERE spec_id = ?",
            [SpecStatus.RETIRED.value, reason, spec_id],
        )
        return self.get_spec(spec_id)

## Claude

I checked `paper/store.py` and `paper/engine.py` directly — there's a real implementation detail Cursor's B/D phrasing glosses over, and it changes *where* this hook actually goes.

## The single choke point already exists — but it's not where B/D say it is

`PaperEngine` has exactly one private method, `_journal(self, entry: JournalEntry) -> JournalEntry`, and every `JournalEntry(...)` construction across the file (five separate call sites, inside both `execute_timed_entries` and `run_replay`) funnels through it before being persisted. That's the real, single choke point — not "`PaperStore`'s write path" as B/D phrase it. This matters because hooking the citation-upsert logic at each of the five construction sites individually (instead of the one `_journal()` call) is exactly the kind of duplicated-logic mistake that produces inconsistent behavior later, e.g. if a sixth journal-writing call site gets added in some future engine change and someone forgets to wire the citation hook into it too.

## A real coupling problem with "auto-upsert inside PaperStore's write path"

I checked imports: `paper/store.py` already imports `validate_human_identity` from `research_data.brain.models` — so there's *some* precedent for `paper/` depending on `brain/`, but only on a stateless validator function. Nothing in `paper/` imports `research_data.brain.store.BrainStore` (a stateful class needing its own DuckDB connection), and the existing precedent for cross-store coordination is `scripts/run_quality_momentum_study.py` — the one script that holds references to *both* `BrainStore` and `PaperStore` and calls each directly (`brain.approve_spec(...)`, `paper_store.approve_thesis(...)`). If `_journal()` reached into `BrainStore` directly, `paper/` would gain a new, heavier dependency that doesn't match how the rest of this codebase keeps its stores independent.

**The fix:** inject a callback at `PaperEngine` construction time — `PaperEngine(..., on_lesson_journaled: Callable[[JournalEntry], None] | None = None)` — and have `_journal()` invoke it when `entry.entry_type in {"lesson", "exit"}`. The orchestrating CLI command or script (which already constructs both stores, exactly like the study script does today) supplies the closure: `lambda entry: brain.add_citation(build_citation_from_journal(entry))`. `paper/` still never imports `BrainStore` — it only knows about an injected function pointer — preserving the existing separation while guaranteeing the hook fires from the one real choke point regardless of whether the entry came from a live `execute_timed_entries` run or an accelerated `run_replay`. (Worth noting: `execute_timed_entries` returns `list[PaperFill]`, not journal entries, while `run_replay` returns `list[JournalEntry]` — that inconsistency is exactly why hooking `_journal()` itself is more robust than having the orchestration layer try to fish journal entries out of return values after the fact.)

D1's stable-id scheme (`hash("journal_lesson:" + journal_entry_id)`) plugs into this callback with zero changes — `journal_entry_id` is already immutable and unique per entry, so the injected closure is trivially idempotent.

## C's rejection is correctly grounded — confirmed, not just plausible

`run_replay` produces `list[JournalEntry]` over an accelerated historical window that can span years in one execution — meaning a single replay run can legitimately generate dozens of exit entries in seconds. Auto-enqueuing a live CriticReview (an LLM call) per entry would size your API usage by *how much history you replayed*, not by real trading cadence — the "NVDA +939% lesson spam" framing is accurate, not hypothetical, given what `run_replay` actually does.

## Recommendation

**D5: D**, with the implementation corrected to hook `_journal()` via dependency injection rather than "PaperStore's write path."

Suggested reply to Cursor:

> **D5: D**, implementation detail corrected: the hook belongs on `PaperEngine._journal()` — confirmed it's the single choke point every `JournalEntry` construction (five call sites across `execute_timed_entries` and `run_replay`) already funnels through, so hooking there once guarantees the behavior regardless of entry path, instead of risking a future sixth call site being missed.
>
> Wire it as an injected callback, not a hard import: `PaperEngine(..., on_lesson_journaled: Callable[[JournalEntry], None] | None = None)`, invoked when `entry.entry_type in {"lesson", "exit"}`. `paper/store.py` already imports `brain.models.validate_human_identity` (a stateless validator), but nothing in `paper/` imports `brain.store.BrainStore` today — a direct import there would be a new, heavier coupling than exists anywhere else in this codebase. The orchestrating CLI command (which already needs both stores, same pattern as `run_quality_momentum_study.py`) supplies the closure at construction time.
>
> `--cite-lesson` flag: default **on** for real live/replay runs (unconditional, since A2 already requires it and the cost is zero — deterministic, idempotent, no LLM per D1). Keep the opt-out specifically for synthetic/dev-fixture replay runs against `tests/synthetic.py` generators, so manual debugging against fake data doesn't pollute the real citation graph — not because production journaling should ever skip it.

## Claude

I read the actual test suite before answering, and this is more grounded than Cursor's phrasing suggests — this codebase already has a formal, numbered convention for exactly this kind of guarantee, and E1 should extend it rather than invent "goldens" as a separate concept.

## The precedent already exists, precisely

`tests/test_property_no_fabrication.py` is literally **"Property 11: No Data Fabrication on Empty Provider Response"** — a hypothesis-driven test that generates *many* random `ProviderFetchResult` instances with empty records and asserts `MISSING` status results, every time, across the generated space. `tests/test_property_quality_status.py` is **"Property 5: Quality Status Classification Correctness"** — same pattern, fuzzing inputs against the full precedence order. And `tests/test_kronos_reserved.py` already has `test_no_inference_dependencies_reserved_module` — a structural import-boundary test, same genre C4 proposed for `agents/`. This project already has a house style: numbered properties, hypothesis-fuzzed rather than hand-picked, tied to formal requirement language ("For any X, the Y SHALL Z"). E1's new tests for B3/B4's guarantees should get their own numbered properties in this same series, not a separately-branded "golden test suite" — consistency with how trust is already enforced everywhere else in this codebase, not a new convention invented for one layer.

This matters beyond naming: **hand-picked fixtures (a couple of "planted false Sharpe" cases) are exactly the wrong tool for testing a numeric-tolerance boundary.** B3 locked a rounding-epsilon comparison for floats. The bugs that survive code review live *at* that epsilon boundary — a value one unit inside the tolerance vs. one unit outside, a confidence exactly at `max_confidence` vs. one ulp above it. Hypothesis is precisely what this codebase already uses to fuzz boundary conditions (see Property 18's case/whitespace fuzzing of adjustment-policy strings). E1 should do the same: generate many cards with numbers perturbed by varying amounts around the allowed tolerance and assert the validator's accept/reject line falls exactly where B3 said it should — not rely on two or three manually-written "evil card" examples to happen to land on the boundary.

## A real design gap in option C worth closing before it's a validator-only guarantee

Cursor's C says missing/contradictory data must produce `action=INSUFFICIENT_DATA` and the critic "must flag" it. That describes catching a bad output *after* the LLM produces it. But `evidence.py` already has a stronger, cheaper pattern for exactly this situation: `build_evidence_packet` raises `EvidenceConstructionError` and refuses to even construct a packet when provenance is missing — it fails before the risky path is ever entered, not after. The gates use the identical philosophy (`monte_carlo.py`/`oos.py` etc. all "fail closed" before producing a result rather than trusting downstream code to catch a bad one).

The AI hub runner should do the same thing: **when `ScorePacket.data_quality.status` (or `DataEvidencePacket.quality_status`) is `MISSING` or `CONTRADICTORY`, the runner must construct the `INSUFFICIENT_DATA` card deterministically in Python and never call the LLM at all** — not hand it to the analyst and hope it produces the right action label. This is strictly better than "the validator catches it": it's cheaper (no wasted call, no rate-limit budget spent on a call whose answer was already known), and it removes an entire failure class (an LLM — fixture or live — quietly ignoring the quality flag and producing a normal-looking card that then has to be caught downstream). The right test for this isn't "the card says INSUFFICIENT_DATA" — it's a property test asserting **zero invocations of the LLM client** for any packet with `MISSING`/`CONTRADICTORY` status (spy/mock assertion on the call count), mirroring how `EvidenceConstructionError` and the gates' "fails closed" notes work today.

## Recommendation

**E1: D**, with two concrete strengthenings folded in as requirements, not suggestions:

1. New tests are **numbered properties using hypothesis**, matching the existing house style (Property 5, 11, 18, ...) — fuzz-generate cards perturbed around B3's tolerance boundary, don't rely on a handful of hand-picked planted-Sharpe fixtures.
2. Missing/contradictory quality is a **pre-LLM-call guard in the runner**, mirroring `evidence.py`'s `EvidenceConstructionError` pattern — tested by asserting zero LLM-client invocations, not by inspecting the resulting card's action label after the fact.

Suggested reply to Cursor:

Checked `tests/test_property_no_fabrication.py` (Property 11) and `tests/test_property_quality_status.py` (Property 5) — this codebase already has a numbered, hypothesis-fuzzed property-test convention for exactly this class of guarantee. E1's new tests should extend that series (new Property numbers, own namespace since this is the AI-hub phase, not the original `.kiro` ingestion spec), not be a separately-invented "golden" suite. Fuzz the B3 numeric-tolerance boundary with hypothesis (values perturbed just inside vs. just outside the rounding epsilon) rather than relying on a couple of hand-picked planted-Sharpe examples — boundary bugs are exactly what hand-picked fixtures miss and what hypothesis is already the house tool for.
>
> Structural tightening on the missing/contradictory-data requirement (C): don't just validate that the resulting card says `INSUFFICIENT_DATA` after the LLM runs — mirror `evidence.py`'s `EvidenceConstructionError` pattern (and the gates' "fails closed" philosophy) and have the runner **refuse to call the LLM at all** when input quality is `MISSING`/`CONTRADICTORY`, constructing the `INSUFFICIENT_DATA` card deterministically in Python. Test with a property assertion of **zero LLM-client invocations** for such inputs, not just the output's action label — cheaper, and removes an entire failure class instead of catching it downstream.

> Citation-hallucination test (D's addition) follows `test_kronos_reserved.py`'s existing pattern exactly — construct a card citing an `evidence_ref`/`citation_id` absent from the assembler's input and assert rejection, same shape as that file's admission-gate tests.

"""Property-based tests for normalizer price adjustment mapping (Property 18).

Property 18: Normalizer Price Adjustment Mapping
For any provider response with a given adjustment_policy, the Normalizer SHALL
map it to the correct PriceAdjustment enum value:
- "raw", "unadjusted" -> PriceAdjustment.RAW
- "split_adjusted", "split" -> PriceAdjustment.SPLIT_ADJUSTED
- "split_dividend_adjusted", "fully_adjusted", "adjusted" -> PriceAdjustment.SPLIT_DIVIDEND_ADJUSTED
- Anything else -> PriceAdjustment.UNKNOWN

The mapping is case-insensitive (the function lowercases input).

**Validates: Requirements 4.2, 4.4**
"""

import sys

sys.path.insert(0, "src")

from hypothesis import given, settings, assume
from hypothesis import strategies as st

from research_data.models import PriceAdjustment
from research_data.normalization import map_adjustment_policy

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Known policy mappings
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_RAW_POLICIES = ["raw", "unadjusted"]
_SPLIT_POLICIES = ["split_adjusted", "split"]
_SPLIT_DIVIDEND_POLICIES = ["split_dividend_adjusted", "fully_adjusted", "adjusted"]

_ALL_KNOWN_POLICIES = _RAW_POLICIES + _SPLIT_POLICIES + _SPLIT_DIVIDEND_POLICIES

_EXPECTED_MAPPING: dict[str, PriceAdjustment] = {}
for p in _RAW_POLICIES:
    _EXPECTED_MAPPING[p] = PriceAdjustment.RAW
for p in _SPLIT_POLICIES:
    _EXPECTED_MAPPING[p] = PriceAdjustment.SPLIT_ADJUSTED
for p in _SPLIT_DIVIDEND_POLICIES:
    _EXPECTED_MAPPING[p] = PriceAdjustment.SPLIT_DIVIDEND_ADJUSTED

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Hypothesis strategies
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Strategy for known policies with random casing and surrounding whitespace
_known_policy_strategy = st.sampled_from(_ALL_KNOWN_POLICIES).flatmap(
    lambda policy: st.tuples(
        st.just(policy),
        # Apply random case transformations and optional whitespace
        st.builds(
            lambda chars, leading_ws, trailing_ws: leading_ws + chars + trailing_ws,
            # Randomly uppercase each character
            st.builds(
                lambda p, mask: "".join(
                    c.upper() if m else c.lower() for c, m in zip(p, mask)
                ),
                st.just(policy),
  ```

## E2 — Live eval (manual / optional CI job)

**Why it matters:** A3’s live holding card is the desk-is-real bar, but it must not enter default `pytest -q` (network + keys). Live eval compares analyst/critic output to **already recorded** study facts (Phase 2b stdout / DuckDB gate rows), not to a second invented truth. Critic must catch a **planted** false Sharpe in a mutated packet (deterministic evil input + live or fixture critic — prefer validator path already proven offline; live checks “model + structured bind still obey”).

| | Option | Live checks | Where it runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Skip live eval; offline Properties only | None | CI only |
| **B** | Manual script: assemble real packets for one symbol (NVDA/GOOGL/AAPL) → `analyze-symbol` → assert card numbers ⊆ allowlist vs ScorePacket/gate projection; blind-diff key floats vs study report | Desk smoke | Human / `scripts/` not pytest |
| **C** | **B** + inject mutated gate summary (false Sharpe) into critic input; expect CriticReview reject / fail | Adversarial live | Same |
| **D** | Nightly GitHub Action with secrets | Automation | Quota/cost; optional later |
| **E** | Live calls inside default pytest | Convenience | **Forbidden** |

**Recommended default: C** as the A3 DoD procedure (documented in Phase 3 SoT + runbook), implemented as `scripts/live_ai_card_smoke.py` or CLI flags `--live` gated on env — **not** in default pytest. **B** alone is the minimum; **C** matches your A3 “critic artifact” + B3 planted-false-Sharpe intent under real provider. **D** deferred until volume justifies Azure reserve.

**Bad choice breaks:**
- **A** → never proves Gemini/instructor path on real DuckDB.
- **E** → CI nondeterminism + secret leakage risk.
- **D too early** → burns free-tier / ops before schema stabilizes (B2 build #2 lesson).

## Claude

I checked whether option D's "linked thesis" premise actually holds up in the current models before answering — it doesn't quite, and the fix is smaller than either C or D as literally written.

## D assumes a linkage that doesn't exist yet

D says the JSONL writer fires "when human closes a **linked** thesis." I checked `Thesis` (`paper/models.py`): it already has `spec_id: str | None = None` — *"strategy spec this thesis came from, if any"* — an established precedent for linking a `Thesis` back to whatever upstream artifact justified it. But there is **no equivalent field linking a `Thesis` to the `EvidenceCard` that informed it.** Without that field, D's JSONL writer has nothing reliable to key off — the only way to guess which card "informed" a given thesis would be matching on symbol + rough date proximity, which is fragile: a human could read a card, sit on it for three weeks, then open a thesis for reasons that drifted from what the card actually said. That's not a join key, it's a guess dressed up as one — and building outcome-tracking infrastructure on a guessed join is worse than not building it, because it *looks* like provenance without actually being able to attribute an outcome to a specific card version.

## The actual fix costs almost nothing and is irreversible if skipped

Add one field, following the exact pattern that already exists: **`Thesis.source_card_id: str | None = None`**, same idiom as `spec_id`, zero new design concept. This is cheap for a very concrete reason: no real `Thesis` rows exist yet from AI-hub cards (V1 hasn't shipped), so there's no backfill, no migration pain, nothing to reconcile — it's a free column addition today and an impossible retrofit later, because you cannot reconstruct three months from now which card a human happened to be looking at when they opened a position. This is the same logic already applied twice in this conversation: D1's stable citation-id (capture identity cheaply now, defer the expensive part) and B2's "don't freeze the big table early, but get the schema field right while it's free."

With that one field in place, **you don't need D's JSONL file at all.** A future calibration job can join entirely within stores that already exist: `JournalEntry.thesis_id → Thesis.source_card_id → EvidenceCard`. No new file format, no new directory under `data/cards/outcomes/`, no new writer. D's stated goal (a future join key) is fully achieved with less mechanism than D itself proposes.

## Why deferring the math specifically is correct — not just "no data yet"

Cursor's rationale ("mapping WATCH→binary outcome is ambiguous") understates the actual gap. Brier scoring requires a **predicted probability of a specific binary event** and an **observed 0/1 outcome**. This project's action labels aren't that — `ACCUMULATE` isn't "P(price rises)=0.7," it's a qualitative stance, and `confidence` is a quality-capped trust ceiling, not a calibrated probability of any named event. Before any Brier math can exist, someone has to define what's actually being predicted (P(outperforms VOO over N sessions)? Over what N?) — that's an unanswered design question, not an engineering gap. Building the math now would mean answering a question that hasn't been precisely asked yet. This belongs in the north-star doc as an explicitly flagged open question, not quietly assumed away by picking a table shape.

## Recommendation

**E4: C, plus one field** — not a full D. Defer all calibration math and any new outcome-artifact pipeline; add `Thesis.source_card_id: str | None = None` now, at zero cost, because it's the one piece that's actually unrecoverable if skipped.

This is the one piece that's genuinely irrecoverable if skipped: you cannot retroactively know which card a human was reading when a thesis opened.

## Claude

Good instinct — "C" as a label doesn't specify enough to actually build from. Here's the concrete module map, written in the same documented style as `YEAR_AHEAD_BASE.md` so it can drop straight into that doc.

## Structure principle

Two packages, one hard rule between them: **`cards/` holds every deterministic thing (schema, math, file I/O) and imports zero LLM libraries. `agents/` holds every LLM-touching thing and is the only package allowed to import `litellm`/`instructor`/etc.** Everything already locked in B1–E4 gets a named home below — nothing here is new scope, it's just giving each already-decided piece its own file so it's independently testable and scannable at a glance, matching how `gates/` already splits `oos.py`/`monte_carlo.py`/`walk_forward.py`/`deflated_sharpe.py` into one file per concern instead of one big module.

```text
src/research_data/
  cards/                    # Deterministic schema + validators — ZERO LLM imports (C4 boundary)
    models.py               #   EvidenceCard, CriticReview (separate model, B2), schema_version
    gate_projection.py      #   GateSummaryProjection: live gate keys (oos_sharpe, tail_annualized_return,
                             #   fraction_positive, deflated_sharpe_probability) -> whitelist names (B1 logistics)
    allowlist.py             #   NumericAllowlist builder: ScorePacket + GateSummaryProjection + journal floats;
                             #   tolerance-based float match, exact int match (B3)
    validators.py             #   post-validators: allowlist check, confidence <= cap clamp + critic-monotone (B4),
                             #   citation/evidence_ref existence check (E1's hallucination-reject target)
    writer.py                #   JSON writer: data/cards/{symbol}_{as_of}_{card_id}.json (B2 build #1)
    store.py                 #   [build #2, later] DuckDB evidence_cards table, mirrors raw_market_payloads
                             #   hash+path shape — file reserved/named now, empty until B2's "goldens stable" gate

  agents/                   # The ONLY package allowed to import litellm/instructor/pydantic_ai (C4)
    llm_client.py             #   litellm.Router: Gemini Flash -> Groq -> local Ollama fallback (C3/C4-impl)
    assemble.py               #   packet assembler; pre-LLM guard: MISSING/CONTRADICTORY quality -> build
                             #   INSUFFICIENT_DATA card in Python, zero LLM calls (E1) — own file so this
                             #   guard is testable (assert zero client invocations) without mocking analyst/critic
    analyst.py                #   Analyst prompt + instructor/pydantic-ai binding -> EvidenceCard draft
    critic.py                 #   Critic prompt + binding -> CriticReview draft (confidence_delta <= 0 only)
    runner.py                 #   orchestrates assemble -> analyst -> cards.validators -> critic -> cards.writer;
                             #   fixture provider path when RESEARCH_DATA_LLM=fixture (offline default)

  brain/                    # UNCHANGED location. models.py gains params_delta/parent_spec_id (D2) — a field
                             # addition to an existing file, not new scope. Still zero LLM imports.
  paper/                    # UNCHANGED location. models.py gains Thesis.source_card_id (E4) — field addition.
                             # engine.py gains injected on_lesson_journaled callback param (D5) — no new import.
  factors/ gates/ fundamentals/ strategies/   # UNCHANGED — untouched by any of this

  cli.py                    # Existing: init-db / ingest-prices / audit-prices / benchmark

                             # + NEW: analyze-symbol / critique-spec (D4) — calls agents.runner only, no provider import here
                             # + NEW: cite-add / cite-from-vault / cite-from-journal (D1) — deterministic, no LLM

scripts/

  live_ai_card_smoke.py            # NEW (E2) — env-gated live smoke; not in default pytest

data/

tests/

Live symbol leans **NVDA** (not yet formally confirmed) |\n## Block B — Packet Contracts\n*Mechanism:* the LLM may only ever see structured packets already computed by deterministic Python — never raw OHLCV, never Kronos, never anything it could use to invent a number.\n| ID | Decision | Key notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| **B1** | **D + amendments** | Analyst quotes numbers from **`ScorePacket`** only; `DataEvidencePacket` contributes `evidence_refs`/citation handles only — its `PacketDataQuality` and the packet's own `quality_status`/`confidence_cap` are not both serialized (real overlap found by reading `factors/packets.py` + `models.py:269`). Critic gets a fixed whitelist projection of `TestRunRecord` (`oos_net_sharpe`, `mc_p5_return`, `wf_pct_positive`, `deflated_sharpe_probability`) via a new **`GateSummaryProjection`** — not the raw `inputs`/`outputs` dict, which is untyped `dict[str, Any]` per gate module today. Live gate keys today are `oos_sharpe`, `tail_annualized_return`, `fraction_positive`, `deflated_sharpe_probability` — the projection renames, never recomputes. Plus `PromotionDecision` + relevant `JournalEntry`s. Kronos never attached. Raw `FundamentalsSnapshot` only if `ScorePacket` marks `INSUFFICIENT_DATA` |\n| **B2** | **B** — Pydantic `EvidenceCard` in `cards/models.py` + `schema_version`; CLI writes `data/cards/{symbol}_{as_of}_{card_id}.json`; goldens on fixtures; vault YAML becomes a mirror example, not SoT; **`CriticReview`** is a separate model, not jammed into the card | *Sequencing (not reopened):* DuckDB `evidence_cards` table is **build #2**, started only after goldens pass and the live A3 card hasn't needed a breaking field change. Mirrors `raw_market_payloads`'s existing hash+path pattern (`card_id` PK, `card_path`, `symbol`, `as_of`, `schema_version`, nullable `spec_id`) — this repo has no migration mechanism (schema changes = backup + rebuild the DuckDB file, per the existing `.bak-*` files), so committing the table before the card shape stabilizes means rebuilding two artifacts instead of one |\n| **B3** | **D** — post-validator (allowlist) + critic (adversarial second pass), built from `ScorePacket` + `GateSummaryProjection` + journal floats | The allowlist matcher must use **tolerance-based numeric matching, not exact string containment** — this repo already renders floats rounded (`walk_forward.py`, `cli.py`, `benchmark.py` all format to 2–6 decimals), so a true card will never literal-match a raw `ScorePacket` float. Two typed buckets: float performance numbers matched within a rounding epsilon (pinned in the golden tests themselves); structural ints (`rank`, `universe_size`, `ranked_count`, `window_sessions`, `overlapping_sessions`, `price_rows_used`) matched exactly, no tolerance |\n| **B4** | **D** — hard clamp + critic monotone | Confidence cap SoT = `ScorePacket.data_quality.max_confidence` only. Reject `confidence > max_confidence`. `CriticReview.confidence_delta ≤ 0` (critic may only lower, never raise). Final confidence = `min(analyst, critic-adjusted, cap)` |\n## Block C — Agent Topology\n*Mechanism:* thin roles bound to packets, not a multi-agent debate deciding outcomes.\n| ID | Decision | Key notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| **C1** | **B** — Analyst + Critic only | Researched TradingAgents (arXiv 2412.20138, TauricResearch repo) in depth before deciding. Its Trader and Portfolio-Manager roles are **LLMs that approve the trade directly** — the one thing `brain/models.py`'s `validate_human_identity` (and the fixed `_NON_HUMAN_IDENTITIES` set, which literally includes `\"cursor\"`/`\"claude\"`/`\"fable\"`) exists to forbid. Importing that topology hands promote/demote authority to a debate instead of the four gates + `anant`. Declined to reopen the session's own \"no multi-agent debate as edge\" rule. **Tutor** (decision-inert explanation pass over a finalized card) deferred to V1/V1.1, since it never touches confidence/gates. **Research-proposer swarm** (parallel agents drafting `Citation`s + `PROPOSED` `StrategySpec`s from external research) deferred as its own future block — feeds the existing human-gated loop, never confidence/gates/promote directly |\n| **C2** | **E** — plain Python library runner (`agents/runner.py`, path locked) + thin Typer CLI wrappers | **No LangGraph.** The pipeline is a fixed two-LLM-call linear sequence with no model-decided branching — LangGraph solves dynamic/looping agent-flow problems this pipeline doesn't have, and would be the first dependency in a project whose entire stack today is `pydantic`/`duckdb`/`typer`/`hypothesis`/`pytest`/`exchange_calendars` (checked `pyproject.toml` directly). Implementation: **`instructor`** or **`pydantic-ai`** to bind LLM output directly to `EvidenceCard`/`CriticReview` schemas; **`litellm`** as the provider call interface; a fixture provider mirroring the existing `csv_fixture` pattern for offline CI |\n| **C3** | **E** — `litellm` + fixture offline default | Confirmed `litellm`'s SDK is free/open-source (you only pay the underlying provider; the paid Proxy/Gateway/Enterprise tiers are a separate, unneeded product). Researched current free-tier terms directly: **live default = Gemini Flash** (`LLM_MODEL=gemini/<current-flash-alias>`, 1,500 req/day free, native JSON schema, no card). **Alternate = Groq** (`groq/llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, JSON mode + tool calling on all models, 1,000 req/day free). **GitHub Models excluded** — retires 2026-07-30. **Azure OpenAI ($100 credit) held in reserve**, not the V1 path — new subscriptions default to 0 TPM quota requiring an explicit increase-request with real turnaround (confirmed via Microsoft's own quota docs), not worth the ramp-up for this call volume. `.env.example` must disclose that Google's free tier may use prompts for training (low-stakes for public market data, stated explicitly rather than assumed) |\n| **C4** | **E** — LLM stack importable only under `agents/`; sole `litellm` import site = `agents/llm_client.py` | Extend `test_security_scope.py` to fail on `litellm\\|openai\\|anthropic\\|instructor\\|pydantic_ai\\|google.generativeai` anywhere else under `src/research_data/`. **Implementation, not new scope:** `agents/llm_client.py` uses `litellm.Router` (confirmed available in the open-source SDK, not gateway-only) with an ordered fallback chain — Gemini Flash → Groq → local Ollama — so one provider's rate limit never blocks the runner. Confirmed via clarifying question that \"running almost all the time hunting for information\" meant robustness for the already-scoped small call volume, **not** a continuously-running background research daemon — that idea stays scoped to the deferred research-proposer swarm (C1), not folded into C4 |\n## Block D — Proposal / Citation Logistics\n*Mechanism:* the compound loop (`citation → proposed spec → human approve → gates → promote/demote → journal lesson → next citation`) without an LLM proposer yet.\n| ID | Decision | Key notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| **D1** | **E** (A+B+D; C — LLM claim extraction — deferred with the research-proposer swarm) | Deterministic CLI (`cite-add`, `cite-from-vault`, `cite-from-journal`), outside `agents/`. **Stable citation id = `hash(vault_relpath + content_hash(claims_section))`, not mtime.** Checked `brain/store.py`: `Citation.citation_id` defaults to a random UUID4 and the store has no `update_citation` — insert-only. So a note edit must produce a **new** citation row (old one stays untouched, so any past `PromotionDecision.evidence_citation_ids` keeps pointing at what was actually cited at decision time) — mtime-based hashing would either false-trigger on a no-op touch or, if implemented as an in-place update, silently mutate evidence behind a past decision. `cite-add` (manual) **warns**, does not block, on a normalized title/author match against `list_citations()` — nothing today prevents accidental duplicate literature entry. **Claims: empty allowed at ingest, non-empty required only at use** (when assembling `evidence_citation_ids` for a `PROPOSED` spec or `PromotionDecision`) — not a `Citation` model length validator |\n| **D2** | **D, tightened** | Standing contract for any `PROPOSED` `StrategySpec` (human-authored in V1; an LLM proposer inherits the same contract in V1.1 — V1 AI still emits no specs, only `CriticReview`). **`n_trials` is already auto-counted today** via `BrainStore.count_tested_specs()` (`COUNT(DISTINCT spec_id) FROM brain_test_runs`) — global, not scoped to a hook family. Corrected Cursor's own draft, which proposed scoping the count to \"specs sharing hook family\": that would **under-count** trials and understate DSR's selection-bias deflation exactly as the strategy pack grows across families. **`declared_n_trials` rejected outright** — `Docs/[REDACTED].md`'s own risk register already names \"DSR self-deception via trial accounting\" and mitigates it by deriving the count from recorded runs, never a declared field; a manual override would be the first fabricated-adjacent number in this codebase's gate math. New fields `params_delta` + `parent_spec_id` (provenance only) added to `StrategySpec`; `params` itself stays the fully-merged, self-contained dict computed at propose-time, so `resolve_hook` and gate execution never walk a parent chain at runtime. `resolve_hook(hook_ref)` must succeed at **propose-time**, not just before approve (fail-closed early, matching every other check in this codebase) |\n| **D3** | **D + structural tests, not prose-only** | Traced the actual call paths: `GateHarness()` is invoked with zero config args today, and strategy hooks take `universe` as a caller-supplied positional argument, never read from inside `params` — so the \"backdoors\" this question worried about don't exist *yet*, but only because nothing wires them, not because anything blocks them. Required, not optional: (1) `GateHarnessConfig`/`OOSParams`/`MonteCarloParams`/`WalkForwardParams`/`DeflatedSharpeParams` never constructed with non-default args outside `tests/`; (2) no strategy hook reads `universe`/`symbols`/cost-bps keys from `params`; (3) `kronos_reserved` never imported under `agents/`. Card/critic banned-token list = `BUY`/`SELL`/\"guaranteed\"/\"risk-free\" only — **do not** reuse `benchmark.py`'s `_EXECUTION_TOKENS` verbatim, since it also bans `HOLD`, correct only in that pure-data-reporter context |\n| **D4** | **D** — Typer `brain propose/approve/reject/decide` + `analyze-symbol`/`critique-spec` on `cli.py`, plus a one-way vault mirror, **promoted into V1** (not deferred to F3 as Cursor first suggested) | Confirmed `cli.py` has zero brain commands today (only `init-db`/`ingest-prices`/`audit-prices`/`benchmark`), and `approve_spec`/`reject_spec`/`retire_spec` already call `validate_human_identity` at the store layer — a CLI wrapper adds no new place to bypass the gate. New commands go on `cli.py`'s Typer app, not the study script's `argparse` pattern (`scripts/run_quality_momentum_study.py` is explicitly a one-off study runner per this repo's own module map, not the general desk CLI). **Vault mirror pulled forward into V1** because it's one-directional (DB → vault markdown, read-only) and this entire questionnaire session's own pattern — reading Postmortem, Post Base, Kronos deep-dive, etc. to ground nearly every decision — demonstrates where this desk's actual working context lives. \"DB wins on conflict\" is literal: the vault file is regenerated from the DB row, never the reverse |\n| **D5** | **D** — cite on lesson/exit `JournalEntry`; no auto critic/proposer | Checked `paper/engine.py`: every `JournalEntry` construction (five call sites across `execute_timed_entries` and `run_replay`) funnels through one private method, **`PaperEngine._journal()`** — that is the real single choke point, not \"`PaperStore`'s write path\" as first phrased. Wired as an **injected callback** (`on_lesson_journaled`), not a hard import — `paper/store.py` already imports `brain.models.validate_human_identity` (a stateless validator), but nothing in `paper/` imports `brain.store.BrainStore`, and a direct import there would be new, heavier coupling than anything else in this codebase. The orchestrating CLI (which already needs both stores) supplies the closure. `--cite-lesson` defaults **on** for real runs; opt-out is for synthetic/dev-fixture replays only, so debugging against fake data doesn't pollute the real citation graph |\n## Block E — Eval, Safety, Cost\n| ID | Decision | Key notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| **E1** | **D, tightened** | This repo already has a formal, numbered, hypothesis-fuzzed property-test convention (`test_property_no_fabrication.py` = Property 11, `test_property_quality_status.py` = Property 5). E1's new tests extend that series (own AI-hub namespace) rather than being a separately-invented \"golden\" style — fuzzing the B3 float-epsilon tolerance boundary with `hypothesis`, not just a couple of hand-picked planted-Sharpe fixtures. **Structural tightening on missing/contradictory data:** the runner must **never call the LLM at all** when `ScorePacket`/`DataEvidencePacket` quality is `MISSING`/`CONTRADICTORY` — Python builds the `INSUFFICIENT_DATA` card deterministically, mirroring `evidence.py`'s `EvidenceConstructionError` / the gates' fail-closed pattern. Tested by asserting **zero LLM-client invocations**, not just the output's action label. Citation-hallucination rejection follows `test_kronos_reserved.py`'s existing \"construct a bad object, assert rejection\" shape |\n| **E2** | **C** — live smoke = real-packet allowlist check + planted false-Sharpe critic-reject | Documented in the Phase 3 SoT/runbook; `scripts/live_ai_card_smoke.py` and/or a `--live` env-gated CLI flag — **not** in default `pytest`. Nightly GitHub Action (option D) deferred until call volume justifies it. Live symbol still open, leans **NVDA** |\n| **E3** | **C** | `.env.example` gains `LLM_MODEL`, `GEMINI_API_KEY=`, `GROQ_API_KEY=`, optional `OLLAMA_API_BASE`, `RESEARCH_DATA_LLM=fixture\\|live`, and the training-disclaimer comment. The assembler sends `ScorePacket` + `evidence_refs` + the gate whitelist projection — **never raw `daily_ohlcv` bars** (cost, training exposure, and invention surface, all rejected). `max_tokens` + `litellm.Router` fail-fast pinned in `llm_client.py`. Live smoke prints pass/fail only at default verbosity, not full prompt bodies — mirrors the ingestion path's own `redact_secrets` spirit |\n| **E4** | **C + one field, not full D** | No Brier math, no JSONL outcomes file, no new calibration table in V1. Checked `Thesis` (`paper/models.py`): it already has `spec_id: str \\| None` linking to an originating `StrategySpec`, but nothing links a `Thesis` to the card that informed it. Added **`Thesis.source_card_id: str \\| None = None`**, mirroring the exact existing `spec_id` pattern — free right now (no AI-hub `Thesis` rows exist yet, no migration) and irrecoverable if skipped, since there is no way to retroactively know which card a human was reading when a thesis opened. With that field, a future calibration job joins `JournalEntry.thesis_id → Thesis.source_card_id → EvidenceCard` entirely within existing stores — no new artifact needed, so Cursor's `data/cards/outcomes/` JSONL proposal was dropped as unnecessary mechanism. North-star doc must flag explicitly, not just as \"no data yet\": Brier scoring needs a **named binary predicted event** and a **calibrated probability** — action labels and `confidence` aren't that yet, and defining what's actually being predicted (outperform VOO over what horizon?) is an unresolved design question independent of data volume |\n## Block F — Repo / Vault Structure\n| ID | Decision | Key notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| **F1** | **C**, with the concrete module map locked | `cards/` (`models.py`, `gate_projection.py`, `allowlist.py`, `validators.py`, `writer.py`, `store.py` reserved-but-empty for B2's build #2) — zero LLM imports, one file per concern, mirroring how `gates/` already splits one file per gate rather than one mega-module. `agents/` (`llm_client.py`, `assemble.py`, `analyst.py`, `critic.py`, `runner.py`) — the only package allowed to import `litellm`/`instructor`/`pydantic_ai`; `assemble.py` is split out from `analyst.py` specifically so E1's pre-LLM guard is independently testable. `brain/`, `paper/`, `factors/`, `gates/`, `fundamentals/`, `strategies/` stay at their current locations — only small field additions to existing files (`StrategySpec.params_delta`/`parent_spec_id`; `Thesis.source_card_id`; `PaperEngine`'s injected callback param), no purity impact |\n| **F2** | **D** | `Docs/[REDACTED].md` + `Docs/[REDACTED].md` + `Docs/PHASE3_AI_BRAIN_RUNBOOK.md` + this note as the north-star vault SoT; the dated live-card study write-up happens only after the live smoke (E2) passes, not before |\n## Vault Rearrange (F3)\nExecuted alongside this note, per explicit instruction: plain folder names (no `NN_` numeric prefixes), moves only — nothing deleted. `20_Progress/Projects/CS/TradingView/` now holds five subfolders:\n- **Canon/** — settled, do-not-reopen architecture and law: `Year-Ahead Base — Fable 5 Architecture Contract`, `Postmortem - Stocks-ETFs First, Prediction Markets Second`, `Math-First Map — Existing Code to Factor Brain`\n- **Session Findings/** — dated Q&A/decision logs, newest wins on conflict: `Session Findings — Cursor Alignment Pass (2026-07-10)`, `Session Findings — Post Base (2026-07-11)`, this note\n- **Phases/** — landed-work records: `Phase 2 — Strategy Pack Landed (2026-07-11)`\n- **Research/** — pre-Canon research, product vision, and brainstorm notes that fed the settled design: the `AI Market Analyzer - *` set, `Trading with Ai`, `Trading Tools and Platforms`, `Trading`, `RESEARCH`, `Reddit`, `Links`, and the three `Research - *` deep-dives\n- **Archive/** — resolved or superseded, kept for the audit trail, not deleted: `AI Market Analyzer - 4 Month Build Plan` (already `status: archived`), `History Depth Blocker — Massive Starter Required` (resolved — Tiingo replaced the Massive-upgrade plan), `Phase 2b — Promotion Study (Draft)` (superseded — the actual study ran and landed; see `Docs/[REDACTED].md` in the repo)\n`Fable 5 — Read Order (TradingView folder)` stays at the folder root as the navigation index and has been rewritten to point here.\n## Open Items\n- [ ] Confirm the A3/E2 live symbol (leaning NVDA; GOOGL/AAPL were the other candidates from the demo_eligible pack)\n- [ ] G1–G3 (Cursor vs Fable split, Fable one-shot scope, explicit non-goals) — not yet answered\n- [ ] Write the Fable 5 implementer prompt once G-series closes\n- [ ] Confirm current Gemini Flash model alias in Google's docs at implementation time (aliases get renamed/versioned)\n## Related\n- [[Session Findings — Post Base (2026-07-11)]]\n- [[Postmortem - Stocks-ETFs First, Prediction Markets Second]]\n- [[Year-Ahead Base — Fable 5 Architecture Contract]]\n- [[Phase 2 — Strategy Pack Landed (2026-07-11)]]\n- Repo: `CLAUDE.md`, `Docs/YEAR_AHEAD_BASE.md`, `src/research_data/brain/`, `src/research_data/paper/`, `tests/test_security_scope.py`"}`

Newer Session Findings beat older notes on conflict.==\n## Goal\nGive Fable 5 a two-minute path into the current base and the AI-hub design pass, without re-deriving either.\n## Folder map\n`20_Progress/Projects/CS/TradingView/` now holds five subfolders, plain names, no numeric prefixes: **Canon/** (settled architecture and law), **Session Findings/** (dated decision logs, newest wins), **Phases/** (landed-work records), **Research/** (pre-Canon research and product vision), **Archive/** (resolved or superseded, kept for audit trail — never deleted).\n## Read order\n1. [[Session Findings — AI Brain Hub (2026-07-12)]] — **current SoT**; AI analyst/critic harness design (A1–F2) on top of the `demo_eligible` pack\n2. [[Session Findings — Post Base (2026-07-11)]] — prior SoT for ingestion/Phase 2b facts; still valid where the AI Brain Hub note doesn't override it\n3. ## Claude

Before locking G1, let me check one thing: does the "Cursor builds everything except the actual LLM call" shape have precedent in this codebase, or is it a novel pattern being invented for this handoff?

Confirmed — this isn't a new pattern being invented for the Cursor/Fable split, it's already proven in this exact codebase.

## The precedent: `kronos_reserved.py` is the same shape as Bundle D

Its own docstring: *"RESERVED architecture only. No inference... deliberately imports no model code and downloads nothing."* That module builds the complete contract — schema, admission gates, the exact shape a forecast must satisfy — around a seam that will eventually call an external model, without ever calling it. Bundle D asks Cursor to do precisely this for the analyst/critic seam: build every deterministic piece around the LLM call (schema, allowlist, assembler, pre-LLM guard, CLI wiring, structural tests) and stop exactly at the boundary where a real `litellm.completion()` call would go. This isn't a hypothetical division of labor — it's the same pattern this codebase already uses successfully for Kronos, applied to a second seam.

## Two corrections worth making before locking D

**1. "Brain Typer stubs" undersells what Cursor actually ships.** `propose`/`approve`/`reject`/`decide` call directly into `BrainStore.propose_spec`/`approve_spec`/`reject_spec`/`record_decision` — methods that already exist today and already enforce `validate_human_identity`. There's no missing piece for Fable to fill in later; wiring Typer around an existing, fully-functional store method is complete work, not a stub. Only `analyze-symbol`/`critique-spec` are genuinely partial — they work end-to-end for the `INSUFFICIENT_DATA` guard path (per E1) but have no happy-path output until Fable's `analyst.py`/`critic.py`/`llm_client.py` exist. Worth naming that distinction explicitly so Cursor doesn't under-build the brain commands thinking they're placeholders too.

**2. E1's Property tests can't actually be written yet — a real number is missing.** B3 locked "tolerance-based float matching, epsilon pinned in the golden tests" but no session decision has ever set an actual epsilon value. This matters concretely: I checked earlier that this codebase already has an established display-rounding convention — `walk_forward.py`/`cli.py` round to `.2f`, `benchmark.py` rounds to `.6f` — and the correct epsilon depends on how `cards/writer.py` formats numbers in card prose (a percentage shown as "62%" tolerates a much wider float delta than one shown as "62.34%"). This is a genuine open sub-decision inside Cursor's `cards/allowlist.py` work, not something Fable can quietly resolve later — without it, the E1 property tests in Bundle C have no concrete boundary to fuzz around.

"""Kronos foundation model — RESERVED architecture only. No inference.

Kronos (Tsinghua, arXiv 2508.02739, AAAI 2026 — not Amazon Chronos) is a
K-line foundation model that MAY later become one evidence input. This
module reserves the contract it must satisfy; it deliberately imports no
model code and downloads nothing.

Admission gates (both mandatory, per the Kronos deep-dive note 2026-06-25):

1. Input quality must be USABLE — PARTIAL/STALE/worse data is never fed to a
   forecaster; the pipeline surfaces INSUFFICIENT_DATA instead.
2. Validated RankIC on THIS universe must be >= 0.03. Until a validation
   pass produces that number, no Kronos output may appear in evidence, and
   nothing Kronos says may influence promote/demote decisions.

A forecast without ``model_rankic_on_universe`` populated is unrepresentable
by design: untested model predictions cannot surface.
"""

## What's missing, with exact grounding for each

1. **No execution language** (`BUY`/`SELL`/"guaranteed"/"risk-free") isn't named anywhere in G3's list, despite D3 having just locked the precise banned-token set for cards/critic output *this session*. This is the single most repeated guardrail in the whole project (`CLAUDE.md`, `benchmark.py`'s `_EXECUTION_TOKENS`, D3) — leaving it off the final checklist because it's "obviously already true" is exactly the assumption a fresh Fable session won't share.
2. **No data fabrication / confidence exceeding the quality cap** — this is the umbrella principle B3, B4, and E1 collectively exist to enforce (numeric allowlist, hard confidence clamp, zero-LLM-call guard on `MISSING`/`CONTRADICTORY`), but it's never stated as its own non-goal bullet — only its individual mechanisms are. Worth one explicit line so it reads as a product law, not just three separate implementation details.
3. **No intraday/tick/options/futures/crypto/margin/leverage** — this is explicit, standing guardrail language in `CLAUDE.md` ("no intraday, tick, options, futures, crypto, margin, or leverage code paths in this phase") that's a genuinely different axis from "no PM/Kalshi" (that's about a second *asset class*; this is about *trading mechanics* even within stocks/ETFs). Nothing in this AI-hub questionnaire touched it because the whole session assumed daily OHLCV — but a Fable session building a "self-improving brain" with fresh eyes could plausibly propose an intraday signal as a "quick win" without this line.
4. **No LLM imports outside `agents/`** as its own restated non-goal, not just folded into "no gate-constant edits" — C4/D3's structural tests are the mechanism, but the *non-goal* itself ("no LLM in ingestion, factors, gates, brain, or paper stores") deserves its own line since it's the single guardrail this entire Block C/D was built around.
5. **TradingView.com / real-trade record-keeping stays out of this build** — `YEAR_AHEAD_BASE.md` settled law #5 states this explicitly and separately from "no broker SDKs" (broker = automated order routing; this = even a manual real-money journal surface). Worth naming distinctly since "no broker" alone doesn't cover it.
6. **Gate order, not just gate constants, is structurally fixed** — I checked `GateHarness.run()`: the OOS→MC→WF→DSR sequence is hard-coded as four sequential method calls, not a configurable/data-driven list. D3's structural tests protect the *constants*; worth one sentence confirming the *order* is equally non-negotiable and equally impossible to reach from any spec/param path today.
7. **Cost-model edits** — currently only implicit inside "no gate-constant edits" (`cost_bps_per_side` is a field on the same dataclasses D3's structural test covers), but D3's own original framing named it as its own reject category. Worth making it an explicit bullet rather than relying on the reader to know it's a gate constant too.

Checked this repo's own commit log: `"Merge pull request #3 from gupta-builds/feat/phase2b-promotion-study"` and `"Merge pull request #2 from gupta-builds/feat/quality-momentum-strategy-pack"` — both landed as PRs opened under the user's own fork/branch and merged after review, not auto-merged by CI and not opened by an agent identity. I've now read every core file this session touched, cross-checked them against the actual live artifacts on disk, and confirmed the full test suite. Here's the real state — not a summary of Cursor's claims, but what I independently verified against the code and its output.

## What's genuinely, verifiably done

**497/497 offline tests pass**, plus the 23 AI-hub-specific tests pass standalone. That's not just "code exists" — I found **real live cards and critic reviews already sitting in `data/cards/`**, produced by actually calling Gemini and Groq (both keys are set in `.env`), not fixtures. One NVDA card I read cites `quality_fcf_score` at 76.4700, momentum rank 11/14, valuation at 102.1619 — all four-decimal-precise, matching `FLOAT_DISPLAY_DECIMALS`, all traceable to real `ScorePacket` fields. A paired `CriticReview` correctly suggests `"demote"` with `confidence_delta: -0.2` and a `gate_summary` whose DSR probability (0.99469988...) matches the actual Phase 2b study's recorded 0.9947 almost to the last digit. This is the real thing working, not a mock.

Every architectural decision I checked traces exactly to its locked answer, several with the decision ID quoted directly in code comments — Fable was working from the actual questionnaire, not reconstructing it: `assemble.py`'s pre-LLM guard (`_BLOCK_LLM_STATUSES = {MISSING, CONTRADICTORY}`) matches E1 exactly; `llm_client.py` is the sole `litellm` import site with the Gemini→Groq→Ollama Router and a documented `max_tokens=8192` reasoning trail for Gemini 3.x's thinking-token budget (C3/C4); `citations.py`'s `vault_citation_id` hashes path+claims-content, never mtime, with a comment citing D1 directly; `brain/store.py`'s `propose_spec` requires non-empty citation claims and calls `resolve_hook` with the comment *"Fail-closed early (D2): resolve at propose-time, not only approve"* — verbatim from my correction. `cards/validators.py`'s banned-token regex has a comment *"NOT benchmark._EXECUTION_TOKENS (which bans HOLD)"* — the exact correction I made at D3. All five D3/C4 structural tests exist and pass (`test_llm_imports_only_under_agents`, `test_kronos_reserved_not_imported_under_agents`, `test_gate_harness_defaults_match_literature`, `test_no_nondefault_gate_params_outside_tests`, `test_strategy_hooks_do_not_read_universe_from_params`). `cards/store.py` is correctly reserved-but-empty for B2's build #2. No LLM-authored `StrategySpec` exists anywhere. `.env.example` even includes the Gemini alias-retirement note I'd flagged as an open item (`gemini-2.0-flash` retired 2026-06-01 → `gemini-3.5-flash`, dated 2026-07-12).

## Two real, small bugs — found by cross-checking live output, not just reading code

1. **`CriticReview.created_at` is model-authored, not server-stamped.** Every single critic review artifact on disk has the identical timestamp `2023-10-27T12:00:00Z` — a hallucinated placeholder the LLM is echoing verbatim across independent calls. `runner.py`'s comment claims *"provenance fields come from the bundle, never from model output"*, but the `model_copy(update={...})` in `run_critique_spec` (and, less visibly, in `run_analyze_symbol`) never actually re-stamps `created_at`. One-line fix in both places.
2. **`cards/validators.py`'s `confidence_values` parameter is dead code** — accepted, looped over, but the loop body is a no-op and the only call site never passes it. Harmless, but should be removed or wired up.

## One thing that's code-complete but not yet empirically exercised

The one-way vault-mirror export (D4) is correctly wired — `write_vault_mirror` fires when a path is supplied, and `scripts/live_ai_card_smoke.py` defaults to `data/cards/{symbol}_live_mirror.md` — but no such file exists on disk yet, meaning the canonical smoke script hasn't been run end-to-end with that default in play (the cards that do exist look like they came from ad-hoc `analyze-symbol`/`critique-spec` CLI calls during development, not the smoke script itself). Worth noting precisely: this mirror is a local markdown file inside the repo, not a direct write into your actual Obsidian vault — Fable has no path to the Windows-side vault, so a human (or I, via the jarvis tools) would need to sync that file in afterward if you want it there.

## Is Phase 3 done?

**Yes — this is a close-out list, not a Phase 3b.** Everything the questionnaire locked is genuinely implemented and, unusually, already validated against a real provider with real output on disk. What's left is small and mechanical:

1. Fix `CriticReview`/`EvidenceCard` `created_at` re-stamping in `runner.py` (two lines)
2. Drop the dead `confidence_values` branch in `cards/validators.py`
3. Run `scripts/live_ai_card_smoke.py` once for real to produce the vault-mirror `.md` file, then decide whether to sync it into the actual vault
4. Merge `feat/phase3-llm-seam` → `main` (your action, per G3's locked policy — matches how PR #2 and #3 actually landed)

None of that needs a redesign, and none of it reopens any locked decision. Once those four are done, Phase 3 is closed and Phase 4 planning (research-proposer swarm, Tutor, `evidence_cards` build #2, PM vertical reconsideration — all already correctly parked as deferred items) is the right next step.

    # -- specs ---------------------------------------------------------------

    def propose_spec(self, spec: StrategySpec) -> str:
        """Store a new spec. It must enter the loop as PROPOSED / UNPROVEN."""
        if spec.status != SpecStatus.PROPOSED:
            raise BrainStoreError(
                f"New specs must have status PROPOSED, got {spec.status.value}"
            )
        if spec.promotion_state != PromotionState.UNPROVEN:
            raise BrainStoreError(
                "New specs must enter with promotion_state UNPROVEN, "
                f"got {spec.promotion_state.value}"
            )
        for citation_id in spec.citation_ids:
            # A proposal must cite research that actually exists in the store.
            citation = self.get_citation(citation_id)
            if not citation.claims:
                raise BrainStoreError(
                    f"Citation {citation_id} has empty claims; "
                    "non-empty claims required when assembling evidence_citation_ids "
                    "for a PROPOSED StrategySpec"
                )
        if spec.parent_spec_id is not None:
            self.get_spec(spec.parent_spec_id)
        if spec.hook_ref:
            # Fail-closed early (D2): resolve at propose-time, not only approve.
            from research_data.brain.loop import resolve_hook

            resolve_hook(spec.hook_ref)

# Copy to .env and fill in. Never commit .env.

# Prices — Massive.com (formerly Polygon.io). NOT polygon.technology (blockchain).
# Dashboard: https://massive.com/dashboard/api-keys
# Either name works; POLYGON_API_KEY is what config/providers.toml reads.
POLYGON_API_KEY=
# MASSIVE_API_KEY=

# Fundamentals (Financial Modeling Prep)
FMP_API_KEY=

# SEC EDGAR identity header (not a secret key — required on every SEC request)
# Format: AppName your.email@example.com
SEC_USER_AGENT=

# Optional backups (not required for the year-ahead base)
# TIINGO_API_KEY=
# ALPHA_VANTAGE_API_KEY=

# --- Phase 3 AI hub (litellm; keys never committed) ---
# RESEARCH_DATA_LLM=fixture|live  (CI/default = fixture; live smoke = live)
RESEARCH_DATA_LLM=fixture
# Documented live default: Gemini Flash free tier.
# Note: Google's free tier may use prompts for product improvement / training —
# low-stakes for public market summary packets, stated explicitly here.
# Alias confirmed 2026-07-12: gemini-2.0-flash was retired 2026-06-01; the
# current litellm alias is gemini/gemini-3.5-flash.
LLM_MODEL=gemini/gemini-3.5-flash
GEMINI_API_KEY=
# Groq fallback (Router: Gemini → Groq → Ollama; used automatically if key set)

GROQ_API_KEY=
# Optional local fallback for litellm.Router (only added when this is set)
# OLLAMA_API_BASE=http://localhost:11434
# LLM_MODEL_OLLAMA=ollama/llama3.1

    @app.command("analyze-symbol")
    def analyze_symbol_cmd(
        symbol: str = typer.Argument(...),
        as_of: Optional[str] = typer.Option(None, "--as-of"),
        quality: Optional[str] = typer.Option(
            None,
            "--quality",
            help="Force quality status for blocked-path tests (missing|contradictory)",
        ),
        cards_dir: str = typer.Option(
            str(project_root / "data" / "cards"), "--cards-dir"
        ),
        vault_mirror: Optional[str] = typer.Option(
            None, "--vault-mirror", help="Optional one-way markdown export path"
        ),
        price_source: Optional[str] = typer.Option(
            "tiingo",
            "--price-source",
            help="Filter daily_ohlcv to one source (default tiingo; required when DB is mixed)",
        ),
        spec_id: Optional[str] = typer.Option(
            None, "--spec-id", help="Attach spec provenance + latest gate batch"
        ),
        db_path: str = typer.Option(default_db, "--db-path"),
    ) -> None:
        """Assemble packets and write an EvidenceCard (blocked + happy paths)."""
        symbol = symbol.upper()
        as_of_date = date.fromisoformat(as_of) if as_of else date.today()
        conn = _open(db_path)
        try:
            if quality is not None:
                status = QualityStatus(quality.lower())
                packet = _minimal_blocked_packet(symbol, as_of_date, status)
                bundle = assemble_symbol_input(score_packet=packet)
            else:
                gate_runs = None
                if spec_id is not None:
                    store = _brain(conn)
                    store.get_spec(spec_id)
                    gate_runs = latest_gate_batch(store, spec_id)
                try:
                    bundle = build_happy_path_bundle(
                        conn,
                        symbol=symbol,
                        as_of=as_of_date,
                        price_source=price_source,
                        spec_id=spec_id,
                        gate_runs=gate_runs,
                    )
                except (RunnerError, StopIteration) as e:
                    typer.echo(str(e) or "no ScorePacket produced", err=True)
                    raise typer.Exit(code=1) from e
            try:
                card = run_analyze_symbol(
                    bundle,
                    cards_dir=cards_dir,
                    vault_mirror_path=vault_mirror,
                )
            except RunnerError as e:
                typer.echo(str(e), err=True)
                raise typer.Exit(code=1) from e
            typer.echo(
                f"card_id={card.card_id} action={card.action.value} "
                f"confidence={card.confidence}"
            )
        finally:
            conn.close()

    @app.command("critique-spec")
    def critique_spec_cmd(
        spec_id: str = typer.Argument(...),
  ```

def main() -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument("--db", default="data/market.duckdb")
    parser.add_argument("--symbol", default="NVDA")
    parser.add_argument("--spec-id", default=DEFAULT_SPEC_ID)
    parser.add_argument("--price-source", default="tiingo")
    parser.add_argument("--cards-dir", default=str(PROJECT_ROOT / "data" / "cards"))
    parser.add_argument(
        "--vault-mirror",
        default=None,
        help="Optional path for one-way DB→markdown mirror of the live card "
        "(default: data/cards/NVDA_live_mirror.md under --cards-dir)",
    )
    args = parser.parse_args()

    from research_data.cli import load_dotenv_if_present

    load_dotenv_if_present(PROJECT_ROOT)

    if os.environ.get("RESEARCH_DATA_LLM", "").strip().lower() != "live":
        print("FAIL: RESEARCH_DATA_LLM=live is required (env-gated smoke)")
        ):
            print("FAIL: packet quality blocks LLM; smoke needs usable data")
            return 1

        from research_data.agents.llm_client import LLMClientError
        from research_data.agents.runner import RunnerError

        # 1) Live analyst card — validators (allowlist, cap, refs, tokens) run inside.
        card = None
        mirror_path = args.vault_mirror
        if mirror_path is None:
            mirror_path = str(Path(args.cards_dir) / f"{symbol}_live_mirror.md")
        try:
            card = run_analyze_symbol(
                bundle,
                cards_dir=args.cards_dir,
                vault_mirror_path=mirror_path,
            )
            assert isinstance(card.action, ActionLabel)
            if round(card.confidence, 2) > round(card.max_confidence, 2):
                raise CardValidationError("card confidence exceeds cap")
